Write a question putting THIS paper to an external expert, and write it ONLY to the output
path named at the end of this task. No preamble, no commentary, no other files. The question
must be self-contained: the expert sees only your text plus main.pdf.

THE PURPOSE OF THIS QUESTION IS DIFFERENT FROM THE PREVIOUS ROUND, AND YOU MUST BUILD IT
DIFFERENTLY.

The previous round asked him, in effect, "is this good enough for the target journal, and if
not, which journal fits?" That question has a built-in downward bias: it invites him to
locate a lower landing place, and that is what it produced. On the one paper of ours where
the question was instead framed as "this is borderline for the higher tier - what would a
referee at that level object to, and what would secure it?", he produced a specific,
named, reachable threshold, we built exactly that, and he then confirmed the threshold met
and the higher venue defensible.

So this question must be a TIER-RAISING INTERROGATION. Its job is to extract a NAMED,
CONCRETE, REACHABLE mathematical target that would move this paper up a tier - not a venue
recommendation.

The question must contain:

 - the paper's main results stated precisely and completely, with hypotheses, in standard
   language, read from the manuscript;
 - what is imported versus what is ours, honestly separated;
 - the priority position as currently understood, including any prior work he or an earlier
   round identified, stated against our own interest;
 - the machine-verification evidence, and a clear statement that numerical batteries are
   sanity checks and never substitutes for proof;
 - his own previous verdict on this paper, quoted or summarised faithfully, INCLUDING the
   parts unfavourable to us. Read artifacts/oracle_sprint_*_referee.md for it. Do not hide
   the rejection or its grounds.

Then ask him these, in this order:

 1. Name the single most valuable NEW RESULT that would move this paper up a tier. Be
    specific: a theorem statement, not a topic. If several, rank them.
 2. For each, is it REACHABLE FROM THE MACHINERY ALREADY IN THIS PAPER? Say which existing
    lemmas, constructions or estimates would feed it, and what genuinely new ingredient is
    missing. If it is not reachable with what we have, say so plainly and say what would be
    needed instead.
 3. Rank the candidates by success probability times tier impact, and give the probability
    as a number, not a word.
 4. Which of these four standard tier-raising levers applies here, and how:
      (a) settling a named open problem in the literature;
      (b) proving something about the FIELD'S OWN standard objects rather than about
          objects this paper defines;
      (c) removing a hypothesis from an existing theorem, especially one the paper
          currently assumes for convenience;
      (d) a sharpness or matching-bound result showing an existing estimate cannot be
          improved.
 5. What is the strongest objection a referee at the higher tier would raise against the
    paper even after that new result is added?

Constraints you must state to him explicitly:

 - Do NOT answer the tier question by recommending a lower-tier journal. We already know
   where this lands as it stands. We are asking what would move it up.
 - Do NOT propose adding more theorems of the kind already present. An earlier round of
   ours mechanically accumulated results and moved nothing; six of our seven papers were
   then found to have priority omissions, because formally new statements composed from
   standard equivalences carry little independent priority. Say this to him.
 - If the honest answer is that no reachable result exists and this paper cannot rise, say
   that plainly and explain what structural feature caps it. A clear, well-argued ceiling
   is a useful answer and will be acted on. A vague encouragement is not.

Tell him a hard, specific negative is more useful than a soft positive, and that every
objection should come with what would resolve it.

## Instruction Priority

Superpowers skills override default system prompt behavior, but **user instructions always take precedence**:

1. **User's explicit instructions** (CLAUDE.md, GEMINI.md, AGENTS.md, direct requests) â€” highest priority
2. **Superpowers skills** â€” override default system behavior where they conflict
3. **Default system prompt** â€” lowest priority

If CLAUDE.md, GEMINI.md, or AGENTS.md says "don't use TDD" and a skill says "always use TDD," follow the user's instructions. The user is in control.

## How to Access Skills

**In Claude Code:** Use the `Skill` tool. When you invoke a skill, its content is loaded and presented to youâ€”follow it directly. Never use the Read tool on skill files.

**In Copilot CLI:** Use the `skill` tool. Skills are auto-discovered from installed plugins. The `skill` tool works the same as Claude Code's `Skill` tool.

**In Gemini CLI:** Skills activate via the `activate_skill` tool. Gemini loads skill metadata at session start and activates the full content on demand.

**In other environments:** Check your platform's documentation for how skills are loaded.

## Platform Adaptation

Skills use Claude Code tool names. Non-CC platforms: see `references/copilot-tools.md` (Copilot CLI), `references/codex-tools.md` (Codex) for tool equivalents. Gemini CLI users get the tool mapping loaded automatically via GEMINI.md.

# Using Skills

## The Rule

**Invoke relevant or requested skills BEFORE any response or action.** Even a 1% chance a skill might apply means that you should invoke the skill to check. If an invoked skill turns out to be wrong for the situation, you don't need to use it.

```dot
digraph skill_flow {
    "User message received" [shape=doublecircle];
    "About to EnterPlanMode?" [shape=doublecircle];
    "Already brainstormed?" [shape=diamond];
    "Invoke brainstorming skill" [shape=box];
    "Might any skill apply?" [shape=diamond];
    "Invoke Skill tool" [shape=box];
    "Announce: 'Using [skill] to [purpose]'" [shape=box];
    "Has checklist?" [shape=diamond];
    "Create TodoWrite todo per item" [shape=box];
    "Follow skill exactly" [shape=box];
    "Respond (including clarifications)" [shape=doublecircle];

    "About to EnterPlanMode?" -> "Already brainstormed?";
    "Already brainstormed?" -> "Invoke brainstorming skill" [label="no"];
    "Already brainstormed?" -> "Might any skill apply?" [label="yes"];
    "Invoke brainstorming skill" -> "Might any skill apply?";

    "User message received" -> "Might any skill apply?";
    "Might any skill apply?" -> "Invoke Skill tool" [label="yes, even 1%"];
    "Might any skill apply?" -> "Respond (including clarifications)" [label="definitely not"];
    "Invoke Skill tool" -> "Announce: 'Using [skill] to [purpose]'";
    "Announce: 'Using [skill] to [purpose]'" -> "Has checklist?";
    "Has checklist?" -> "Create TodoWrite todo per item" [label="yes"];
    "Has checklist?" -> "Follow skill exactly" [label="no"];
    "Create TodoWrite todo per item" -> "Follow skill exactly";
}
```

## Red Flags

These thoughts mean STOPâ€”you're rationalizing:

| Thought | Reality |
|---------|---------|
| "This is just a simple question" | Questions are tasks. Check for skills. |
| "I need more context first" | Skill check comes BEFORE clarifying questions. |
| "Let me explore the codebase first" | Skills tell you HOW to explore. Check first. |
| "I can check git/files quickly" | Files lack conversation context. Check for skills. |
| "Let me gather information first" | Skills tell you HOW to gather information. |
| "This doesn't need a formal skill" | If a skill exists, use it. |
| "I remember this skill" | Skills evolve. Read current version. |
| "This doesn't count as a task" | Action = task. Check for skills. |
| "The skill is overkill" | Simple things become complex. Use it. |
| "I'll just do this one thing first" | Check BEFORE doing anything. |
| "This feels productive" | Undisciplined action wastes time. Skills prevent this. |
| "I know what that means" | Knowing the concept â‰  using the skill. Invoke it. |

## Skill Priority

When multiple skills could apply, use this order:

1. **Process skills first** (brainstorming, debugging) - these determine HOW to approach the task
2. **Implementation skills second** (frontend-design, mcp-builder) - these guide execution

"Let's build X" â†’ brainstorming first, then implementation skills.
"Fix this bug" â†’ debugging first, then domain-specific skills.

## Skill Types

**Rigid** (TDD, debugging): Follow exactly. Don't adapt away discipline.

**Flexible** (patterns): Adapt principles to context.

The skill itself tells you which.

## User Instructions

Instructions say WHAT, not HOW. "Add X" or "Fix Y" doesn't mean skip workflows.

# Brainstorming Ideas Into Designs

Help turn ideas into fully formed designs and specs through natural collaborative dialogue.

Start by understanding the current project context, then ask questions one at a time to refine the idea. Once you understand what you're building, present the design and get user approval.

<HARD-GATE>
Do NOT invoke any implementation skill, write any code, scaffold any project, or take any implementation action until you have presented a design and the user has approved it. This applies to EVERY project regardless of perceived simplicity.
</HARD-GATE>

## Anti-Pattern: "This Is Too Simple To Need A Design"

Every project goes through this process. A todo list, a single-function utility, a config change â€” all of them. "Simple" projects are where unexamined assumptions cause the most wasted work. The design can be short (a few sentences for truly simple projects), but you MUST present it and get approval.

## Checklist

You MUST create a task for each of these items and complete them in order:

1. **Explore project context** â€” check files, docs, recent commits
2. **Offer visual companion** (if topic will involve visual questions) â€” this is its own message, not combined with a clarifying question. See the Visual Companion section below.
3. **Ask clarifying questions** â€” one at a time, understand purpose/constraints/success criteria
4. **Propose 2-3 approaches** â€” with trade-offs and your recommendation
5. **Present design** â€” in sections scaled to their complexity, get user approval after each section
6. **Write design doc** â€” save to `docs/superpowers/specs/YYYY-MM-DD-<topic>-design.md` and commit
7. **Spec self-review** â€” quick inline check for placeholders, contradictions, ambiguity, scope (see below)
8. **User reviews written spec** â€” ask user to review the spec file before proceeding
9. **Transition to implementation** â€” invoke writing-plans skill to create implementation plan

## Process Flow

```dot
digraph brainstorming {
    "Explore project context" [shape=box];
    "Visual questions ahead?" [shape=diamond];
    "Offer Visual Companion\n(own message, no other content)" [shape=box];
    "Ask clarifying questions" [shape=box];
    "Propose 2-3 approaches" [shape=box];
    "Present design sections" [shape=box];
    "User approves design?" [shape=diamond];
    "Write design doc" [shape=box];
    "Spec self-review\n(fix inline)" [shape=box];
    "User reviews spec?" [shape=diamond];
    "Invoke writing-plans skill" [shape=doublecircle];

    "Explore project context" -> "Visual questions ahead?";
    "Visual questions ahead?" -> "Offer Visual Companion\n(own message, no other content)" [label="yes"];
    "Visual questions ahead?" -> "Ask clarifying questions" [label="no"];
    "Offer Visual Companion\n(own message, no other content)" -> "Ask clarifying questions";
    "Ask clarifying questions" -> "Propose 2-3 approaches";
    "Propose 2-3 approaches" -> "Present design sections";
    "Present design sections" -> "User approves design?";
    "User approves design?" -> "Present design sections" [label="no, revise"];
    "User approves design?" -> "Write design doc" [label="yes"];
    "Write design doc" -> "Spec self-review\n(fix inline)";
    "Spec self-review\n(fix inline)" -> "User reviews spec?";
    "User reviews spec?" -> "Write design doc" [label="changes requested"];
    "User reviews spec?" -> "Invoke writing-plans skill" [label="approved"];
}
```

**The terminal state is invoking writing-plans.** Do NOT invoke frontend-design, mcp-builder, or any other implementation skill. The ONLY skill you invoke after brainstorming is writing-plans.

## The Process

**Understanding the idea:**

- Check out the current project state first (files, docs, recent commits)
- Before asking detailed questions, assess scope: if the request describes multiple independent subsystems (e.g., "build a platform with chat, file storage, billing, and analytics"), flag this immediately. Don't spend questions refining details of a project that needs to be decomposed first.
- If the project is too large for a single spec, help the user decompose into sub-projects: what are the independent pieces, how do they relate, what order should they be built? Then brainstorm the first sub-project through the normal design flow. Each sub-project gets its own spec â†’ plan â†’ implementation cycle.
- For appropriately-scoped projects, ask questions one at a time to refine the idea
- Prefer multiple choice questions when possible, but open-ended is fine too
- Only one question per message - if a topic needs more exploration, break it into multiple questions
- Focus on understanding: purpose, constraints, success criteria

**Exploring approaches:**

- Propose 2-3 different approaches with trade-offs
- Present options conversationally with your recommendation and reasoning
- Lead with your recommended option and explain why

**Presenting the design:**

- Once you believe you understand what you're building, present the design
- Scale each section to its complexity: a few sentences if straightforward, up to 200-300 words if nuanced
- Ask after each section whether it looks right so far
- Cover: architecture, components, data flow, error handling, testing
- Be ready to go back and clarify if something doesn't make sense

**Design for isolation and clarity:**

- Break the system into smaller units that each have one clear purpose, communicate through well-defined interfaces, and can be understood and tested independently
- For each unit, you should be able to answer: what does it do, how do you use it, and what does it depend on?
- Can someone understand what a unit does without reading its internals? Can you change the internals without breaking consumers? If not, the boundaries need work.
- Smaller, well-bounded units are also easier for you to work with - you reason better about code you can hold in context at once, and your edits are more reliable when files are focused. When a file grows large, that's often a signal that it's doing too much.

**Working in existing codebases:**

- Explore the current structure before proposing changes. Follow existing patterns.
- Where existing code has problems that affect the work (e.g., a file that's grown too large, unclear boundaries, tangled responsibilities), include targeted improvements as part of the design - the way a good developer improves code they're working in.
- Don't propose unrelated refactoring. Stay focused on what serves the current goal.

## After the Design

**Documentation:**

- Write the validated design (spec) to `docs/superpowers/specs/YYYY-MM-DD-<topic>-design.md`
  - (User preferences for spec location override this default)
- Use elements-of-style:writing-clearly-and-concisely skill if available
- Commit the design document to git

**Spec Self-Review:**
After writing the spec document, look at it with fresh eyes:

1. **Placeholder scan:** Any "TBD", "TODO", incomplete sections, or vague requirements? Fix them.
2. **Internal consistency:** Do any sections contradict each other? Does the architecture match the feature descriptions?
3. **Scope check:** Is this focused enough for a single implementation plan, or does it need decomposition?
4. **Ambiguity check:** Could any requirement be interpreted two different ways? If so, pick one and make it explicit.

Fix any issues inline. No need to re-review â€” just fix and move on.

**User Review Gate:**
After the spec review loop passes, ask the user to review the written spec before proceeding:

> "Spec written and committed to `<path>`. Please review it and let me know if you want to make any changes before we start writing out the implementation plan."

Wait for the user's response. If they request changes, make them and re-run the spec review loop. Only proceed once the user approves.

**Implementation:**

- Invoke the writing-plans skill to create a detailed implementation plan
- Do NOT invoke any other skill. writing-plans is the next step.

## Key Principles

- **One question at a time** - Don't overwhelm with multiple questions
- **Multiple choice preferred** - Easier to answer than open-ended when possible
- **YAGNI ruthlessly** - Remove unnecessary features from all designs
- **Explore alternatives** - Always propose 2-3 approaches before settling
- **Incremental validation** - Present design, get approval before moving on
- **Be flexible** - Go back and clarify when something doesn't make sense

## Visual Companion

A browser-based companion for showing mockups, diagrams, and visual options during brainstorming. Available as a tool â€” not a mode. Accepting the companion means it's available for questions that benefit from visual treatment; it does NOT mean every question goes through the browser.

**Offering the companion:** When you anticipate that upcoming questions will involve visual content (mockups, layouts, diagrams), offer it once for consent:
> "Some of what we're working on might be easier to explain if I can show it to you in a web browser. I can put together mockups, diagrams, comparisons, and other visuals as we go. This feature is still new and can be token-intensive. Want to try it? (Requires opening a local URL)"

**This offer MUST be its own message.** Do not combine it with clarifying questions, context summaries, or any other content. The message should contain ONLY the offer above and nothing else. Wait for the user's response before continuing. If they decline, proceed with text-only brainstorming.

**Per-question decision:** Even after the user accepts, decide FOR EACH QUESTION whether to use the browser or the terminal. The test: **would the user understand this better by seeing it than reading it?**

- **Use the browser** for content that IS visual â€” mockups, wireframes, layout comparisons, architecture diagrams, side-by-side visual designs
- **Use the terminal** for content that is text â€” requirements questions, conceptual choices, tradeoff lists, A/B/C/D text options, scope decisions

A question about a UI topic is not automatically a visual question. "What does personality mean in this context?" is a conceptual question â€” use the terminal. "Which wizard layout works better?" is a visual question â€” use the browser.

If they agree to the companion, read the detailed guide before proceeding:
`skills/brainstorming/visual-companion.md`

# Theorem Inventory

This Stage A inventory was prepared against `scope_contract.md`, `scope_contract.json`, `research_directive.md`, the compiled manuscript sources included by `main.tex`, all `.tex` files in the paper directory, and `references.bib`.

## Summary

The compiled manuscript contains the A/B/C theorem chain: extension-chain forcing, pullback-based local sites and sheafification, terminal-fibre rigidity, lower-fragment separation, component gerbes and gerbe-null semantics, strict and intrinsic visible quotients, pure Ext character blindness, branch-sensitive versus branch-uniform comparison, finite-nerve realization, the torsion-free H2 subgroup classification, bouquet two-branch resolution, the exact obstruction to transferring these results to empirical models, and the split GHZ/Mermin lift.

No required in-scope theorem from the scope contract is completely absent from the compiled manuscript. The main risks are not missing labels but proof-interface sharpness and source hygiene:

- `lem:bouquet-cover-classical-interface` uses `beta` without quantifying it.
- `lem:generated-subsite-model-interface` needs an explicit admissible-model/free-expansion convention.
- `prop:invariance-visible-quotients` must remain groupoid-indexed, not path-independent.
- `sec_forcing_necessity.tex` is an unused stronger draft with duplicate `thm:pointwise-irreducibility` and unresolved references.
- `sequel/` files are out-of-scope split material.
- Several included section files exceed the 800-line discipline.

## Files Written

- `theorem_inventory.json`
- `theorem_inventory.md`

## JSON Inventory

The authoritative machine-readable inventory is in `theorem_inventory.json`. It uses exactly the required top-level keys:

- `valid`
- `in_scope_present`
- `missing_in_scope_results`
- `weak_in_scope_core_results`
- `proof_gaps`
- `supporting_appendix_or_background`
- `out_of_scope_strong_results`
- `split_candidates`
- `irrelevant_or_remove`
- `naive_truncation_risks`
- `journal_style_gaps`

## Classification Notes

The inventory treats the compiled `main.tex` inputs as the current manuscript. It also scans unused `.tex` files because the Stage A prompt requested all TeX sources. Results in `sequel/` and `sec_forcing_necessity.tex` are therefore classified, but not as part of the compiled article.

Classical material is classified as supporting/background when it is a standard sheaf, stack, gerbe, Cech, UCT, finite abelian duality, or finite group structure fact. The paper-specific contribution is the way these tools are assembled into the state-forcing gluing-obstruction and homological-visibility chain.

The inventory records cross-resolution risks only where a future edit might accidentally convert a stated comparison or monotonicity result into a naive invariant. The current manuscript usually handles this correctly by requiring homology-equivalent refinement, chosen zigzags, Leray/good-cover comparison, or explicit pullback hypotheses.

Referee report
Overall decision
Decision on the present submission: reject and invite a substantially reframed resubmission elsewhere.
This is not a rejection on the former APAL grounds. The revised manuscript now states its limitations with unusual clarity: the principal finite-group result is explicitly tied to a chosen good-cover site, finite nerves, a constant finite band, and chosen non-split lift data; it expressly denies that the construction is an invariant of bare empirical models.  It also correctly states that the higher semantic layers are classes of supplied typed expansions, not an existence or closure theorem for pre-existing lower-layer models. 
My rejection is instead based on four remaining matters:


The priority account is not yet safe. The central â€œcomponent gerbe over a global component sectionâ€ construction has a direct Giraud antecedent more exact than the manuscript acknowledges.


Much of the advertised theorem structure is textbook gerbe theory, UCT naturality, finite duality, subgroup arithmetic, or fibre counting. The exact packaging may be new, but the mathematical advance is smaller than the number and presentation of the theorems suggest.


There is a real missing hypothesis in the general finite-nerve ÄŒechâ€“simplicial comparison. The flagship wedge theorem can be repaired without changing its conclusion, but Section 5 is presently stated too generally.


The proofs of the two claims most capable of carrying originalityâ€”the representative-rigid terminal theorem and the prescribed-image prestack constructionâ€”are in the unavailable supplement. The main article itself identifies these as supplement material.  I therefore assess their statements and priority below, but I cannot certify those proofs from main.pdf.


I do not find evidence in the displayed statements of a false flagship finite-group equivalence. The problem is priority, significance, architecture, and one correctable coefficient hypothesisâ€”not an obvious contradiction in the finite-abelian algebra.

No reputable research journal is, in my judgment, more likely than not to accept the current 48+50-page package.

2. Priority audit
Bottom-line priority judgment
The priority narrative is accurate about scope but not yet accurate about mathematical ancestry.
The manuscript now correctly denies a new general classification of gerbes, a presentation-free invariant, a construction from bare empirical models, and an unconditional non-split lifting theorem. That part is satisfactory.
The unsafe part is the claim that the articleâ€™s own contribution includes the component-gerbe construction and a broad family of construction/classification results without identifying how directly those are already contained in classical stack and gerbe theory. There is one definite omitted theorem-level antecedent and several theorem families that are standard lemmas in specialized notation.
I did not find a previously published theorem stating the entire bespoke two-label wedge classification verbatim. That does not make it a substantial priority claim: exact statements manufactured by composing standard equivalences can be formally new while having little independent mathematical priority.
Claim-by-claim audit
2.1 Component-indexed gerbes of a stack with prescribed component sheaf
Nearest prior work: Giraud, Cohomologie non abÃ©lienne, III, Proposition 2.1.5.3.
A modern statement quoting that result says that for any stack S, the projection
SâŸ¶Ï€0â€‹(S)
makes it a gerbe over its sheaf of connected components, and pulling back along a section of Ï€0â€‹(S) gives the corresponding maximal subgerbe. arXiv
That is essentially the structural content of Theorems 4.8(i) and 4.9: the full substack over a global component section is a gerbe. The neutrality/global-object equivalence is standard Giraud classification, and transport under a compatible banded equivalence is standard naturality.
The manuscript itself describes Section 4 as reorganizing the companion paperâ€™s gerbe data and says the local cocycle constructions are standard.  But it does not identify Giraud III.2.1.5.3 at the theorem where the exact construction is used.
Classification: direct omitted antecedent at theorem level. The map
vâŸ¼[E[v]]
is a useful packaging, but not a new construction principle.
Smallest honest repair: add an explicit sentence before Theorem 4.8:

By Giraud III.2.1.5.3, Eâ†’Ï€0â€‹(E) is a gerbe over its component sheaf, and the pullback along a section is the corresponding maximal subgerbe. The following theorem records this standard construction together with the compatibility needed for our later presheaf comparison.

Relabel Theorem 4.8 as a proposition or â€œstandard component-gerbe package.â€ Remove â€œcomponent gerbesâ€ themselves from the originality list. Keep as original only the later matching criterion under the paperâ€™s exact lift hypotheses.
Nothing stronger is required; deletion of the result would be unnecessary.

2.2 Matching without amalgamation versus non-neutral component gerbes
The equivalence is mathematically:


separatedness identifies existence of a matching family with a global section of aF;


global sections of aF index component gerbes;


terminal essential surjectivity identifies existence of a prestack global object with existence of a stack global object;


a component has a stack global object exactly when its gerbe is neutral.


This is correct as an organization of hypotheses, but it is not a new gerbe obstruction theorem. Its novelty lies, at most, in matching these standard facts to the strict presheaf predicate.
The manuscript expressly says that the companion paperâ€™s Theorem 5.20 is the meet-semilattice analogue of Theorem 4.16. 
Classification: paper-specific corollary; substantially present in the cited companion paper.
Smallest honest repair: call it a â€œpresheaf-to-component-gerbe comparison criterion,â€ not a new obstruction classification. Give a four-line dependency diagram displaying the four facts above. State explicitly whether this paper or the companion first contained the result. If the companion predates this paper, the current theorem is a site-theoretic reformulation, not an independent priority claim.

2.3 Terminal-fibre rigidity with strictly pullback-stable representatives
I found no exact published theorem in the targeted search that states your three-way equivalence in precisely this form. That is the most plausible genuinely new technical lemma in the project.
Its ingredients, however, are classical:


choices of pullbacks and split cleavages for fibred categories;


the fact that ordinary pullback choices are generally pseudofunctorial rather than strictly functorial;


classification of A-torsors by H1, with vanishing H1 forcing the relevant Isom torsor to be trivial. Stacks Project+2MathOverflow+2


The strict representative condition is doing real work in the forward direction: equal restricted presheaf sections become literally the same selected representative, so a matching family yields strict descent data. Essential surjectivity then produces a terminal prestack representative. In the converse direction, surjectivity of F(a)â†’aF(a) and H1=0 trivialize the Isom torsor between a chosen representative and an arbitrary terminal stack object.
Classification: plausibly new narrow formulation, but not a new general descent principle.
Smallest honest repair:


State separately which implication uses strict pullback stability.


Cite standard split-fibration/cleavage and H1-torsor inputs.


Put the complete proof in the article, not only the supplement.


Market it as a no-go lemma for overly rigid lifts, not as a new classification of descent.


A stronger theorem would be needed only if you wanted this to carry the paper by itselfâ€”for example, an invariant formulation for pseudofunctorial choices together with an explicit coherence obstruction. That stronger theorem is not necessary for a Cahiers-level paper.

2.4 Explicit cocycle prestacks realizing prescribed H2 classes
The central constructionâ€”local objects, overlap arrows, and a prescribed triple-overlap defectâ€”is standard gerbe theory. Breenâ€™s account is explicitly devoted to constructing cohomology classes from gerbes; modern gerbe treatments also construct local gerbes directly from ÄŒech 2-cocycles. arXiv+2arXiv+2 Waldorfâ€™s lifting-gerbe discussion gives exactly the familiar pattern in which lifts of transition functions fail the cocycle identity by a central ÄŒech 2-cocycle, whose vanishing is equivalent to the liftâ€™s existence. Konrad Waldorf
Consequently:

when H1â€‹(N)=0, prescribing an evaluation homomorphism

its image is a subgroup generated by at most Î² elements, and every such subgroup is the image of some homomorphism from ZÎ².


The paper-specific work is the exact prestack presentation whose presheaf of components has the desired terminal failure, and its insertion into the typed model convention.
Classification: standard cocycle-gerbe realization plus a possibly new finite-label/presheaf packaging.
Smallest honest repair: rename the result:

Do not claim that the construction of the gerbe is new. Claim only the simultaneous control of:

The proof must make those four controls explicit rather than citing â€œstandard constructionâ€ at the point where they are needed.

2.10 Companion-paper overlap
This is an editorially serious unresolved matter.

A referee cannot determine:


which paper has chronological priority;


which proofs are duplicated;


whether the two articles are salami-sliced versions of the same theorem package;


whether one depends logically on the other.


Resolution: before submission, do one of the following:


merge the companion and this paper, deleting duplication; or

If this is not done, rejection for unverifiable self-overlap is justified. A prose assertion that the proofs are included here is not enough.

3. Mathematical and expository objections requiring correction
3.1 The finite-nerve ÄŒechâ€“simplicial comparison is missing connectedness
Proposition 5.6 identifies an ordered ÄŒech n-cochain with one element of A for every nonempty (n+1)-fold intersection, and then identifies that with one coefficient on the corresponding simplex of the ordinary nerve. 
That is not valid merely because the band is trivialized as a constant sheaf. For a disconnected overlap Ui0â€‹â‹¯inâ€‹â€‹,
Î“(Ui0â€‹â‹¯inâ€‹â€‹,Aâ€‹)â‰…AÏ€0â€‹(Ui0â€‹â‹¯inâ€‹â€‹),
not one copy of A. The ordinary nerve records that the overlap is nonempty but not how many connected components it has.
The open-star good covers used in the flagship theorem have contractible, hence connected, nonempty intersections. Thus this does not destroy Theorem 6.16. It does invalidate the generality claimed in Section 5.
Resolution: choose one:


add the hypothesis that every nonempty finite intersection is connected and that the coefficient trivializations identify its section group coherently with A; or


replace the ordinary nerve by the ÄŒech simplicial set/component nerve, with one simplex for each connected component; or


formulate the comparison with the appropriate local coefficient system.


This correction is mandatory. Without it, Section 5 contains a false general assertion.

3.2 The generated-site Leray comparison needs an explicit Comparison Lemma
The proof of Lemma 6.14 passes quickly from contractible open-star intersections to sheaf cohomology of âˆ£Kâˆ£ on the generated site. Contractibility alone does not identify the sheaf category or derived cohomology of an abstractly generated finite-intersection site with the usual topological site.
The required statement is plausible: the open stars and their finite intersections form a basis stable under finite intersections, and the generated coverage should be the induced basis topology. But that equivalence must be stated and proved.
Resolution: add a theorem saying that:


the all-subdivision open-star/intersection category is a basis for XÎ²â€‹;


its generated topology agrees with the induced open-cover topology;


restriction induces an equivalence of sheaf categories;


the paperâ€™s â€œgenuine constant sheafâ€ corresponds to the ordinary constant sheaf;


the relevant intersections are acyclic;


the filtered ÄŒech colimit computes derived H2.


If the supplement already proves exactly this for the all-subdivision site, cite the theorem verbatim in Lemma 6.14 rather than replacing it by two sentences. If it proves only the comparison for one finite intersection site, an additional passage to the common generated site is still needed.

3.3 The presentation-comparison groupoid currently proves too little
The groupoid is freely generated by refinements already assumed to induce H2â€‹-isomorphisms, cocycle replacements, band retrivializations, and inverses. The preservation of KÏ‰â€‹ under those chosen arrows is then UCT naturality. Distinct zigzags may act by different automorphisms, and no path independence is asserted. 
This is legitimate bookkeeping, but not a meaningful invariance theorem. The category has been defined so that the subgroup transports.
Resolution: either:


prove coherence/path independence over a naturally occurring category of presentations; or


demote the groupoid to a definition and one naturality proposition, and remove it from the principal originality list.


For the present paper, the second option is the realistic one.

3.4 The theorem count misrepresents the amount of new mathematics
Examples include:


composition of truth-preserving reducts;


singleton-team agreement;


UCT kernel equivalences;


first-isomorphism and third-isomorphism consequences;


primary decomposition of a quotient;


exact fibre cardinalities;


maximum fibre size for a common auxiliary set.


These are correct, but their theorem-level presentation makes the paper look padded and obscures the few claims that need expert scrutiny.
Resolution: reduce the article to approximately:


one semantic-interface proposition;


one standard component-gerbe proposition;


one matching/non-neutrality theorem;


one terminal-rigidity theorem;


one finite-site realization theorem;


one UCT/aggregate proposition;


the wedge corollary;


the empirical-model boundary theorem.


The rest should be remarks, clauses, or omitted calculations. The theorem count should fall substantially; preserving it is not a virtue.

3.5 The team-semantic architecture is not causally connected to the classification
The upper model class admits arbitrary supplied finite sites, presheaves, prestacks, and stack lifts satisfying the typing conditions. The manuscript correctly admits that this is a model-class convention.  Consequently, the wedge construction is not derived from team semantics. It is inserted into a class broad enough to accept it.
That makes â€œover Teams and Presheavesâ€ an overstatement of what drives the main result.
Resolution: retitle the paper along the lines of:

Component Gerbes and Homological Images on Finite Good-Cover Sites

or

Finite-Site Realizations of Component Gerbes and Homological Quotients

Reduce Sections 2â€“3 to the minimum interface needed for the matching predicate. Keep the lower-fragment separation result in an appendix. This would make the actual mathematical centre visible.
The alternativeâ€”proving that a nontrivial, independently defined class of team-semantic models naturally produces the required non-split liftsâ€”would be substantially new research and is not a realistic revision requirement.

3.6 The â€œauxiliary registerâ€ results should be deleted as a theorem package
Without restrictions on admissible encodings, the minimum size of an auxiliary set needed to inject a fibre of size m is m. The sharpness construction uses arbitrary set-theoretic sections. This is fibre counting, not an information-theoretic theorem.
Resolution: replace all register definitions and theorems with one sentence:

Since the quotient fibres are cosets of the kernel, the minimum cardinality of an unrestricted auxiliary set separating each fibre is the kernel cardinality.

If an actual application imposes locality, equivariance, continuity, computability, or common encoder constraints, a nontrivial result could remain. No such constraint is presently present.

3.7 The 48+50-page architecture is still too large
The old 96-page article has been transformed into a 48-page article plus a 50-page supplement, but the mathematical referee burden remains 98 pages. More importantly, the supplement contains the rigidity and realization proofs that should determine acceptance. 
This is not the same presentation defect as the former opaque terminology, but it remains an editorial obstacle.
Resolution: produce a self-contained article of at most about 40 pages for Cahiers:


integrate the full rigidity proof;


integrate the core prescribed-image construction proof;


remove routine semantic and quotient calculations;


retain a technical appendix only for variants and extended examples.


Dividing into two papers is not attractive because the quotient half is too elementary to stand independently.

3.8 Machine evidence has no bearing on the decision
The finite Python checks are useful regression tests. They do not verify the general theorems, and the inert leanverified markers provide no evidence at all.
Resolution: remove any suggestion in the manuscript or cover letter that these checks support mathematical correctness. A repository note may describe them as sanity checks. Either provide actual Lean artifacts or omit formal-verification language entirely.

4. The NWW extension
Decision: no-go for this paper
The NWW comparison is not within reach of the machinery developed here.
This conclusion is not based on thematic distance. It is based on the exact chain-level gap. The current paper starts after a gerbe H2-class and its ÄŒech representative have been supplied. NWW Problem 8.1(b) asks for a comparison beginning with a strict crossed module and a locally continuous or locally smooth degree-three group cocycle, applying a specific ÄŒechization map, and identifying the resulting ÄŒech 2-cocycle with the lifting-gerbe cocycle.

Thus almost the whole present paper lies downstream of, or orthogonal to, the missing calculation.
How the sign would have to be fixed
The sign cannot be left to UCT or determined by finite testing. It must be fixed by freezing:


the locally smooth group-cohomology differential;


left versus right Q-actions;


the definition of the factor set Îº;


the direction of transition functions kghâ€‹;


the manuscriptâ€™s gerbe defect convention
cijkâ€‹=Î¸ikâˆ’1â€‹Î¸jkâ€‹Î¸ijâ€‹;


the ordered ÄŒech differential.


One then substitutes the characteristic cocycle into NWWâ€™s formula and compares it term by term with zghkâ€‹. That calculation determines + or âˆ’. A separate convention dictionary should show how reversing transition arrows changes the answer.
Extension or separate paper?
Even if completed, this necessarily belongs in a separate paper.
It would introduce:


a new cohomology theory not used elsewhere in the current article;


strict crossed modules and Peiffer identities;


locally smooth group-cohomology representatives;


translated Lie-group covers;


a substantial chain computation;


choice independence and naturality.


That work would be more original than the present article and would change its audience. Adding it would reverse the successful page reduction and produce a manuscript with two unrelated mathematical centres.
Decisive stopping reason
The decisive stopping reason is:

The present paper has machinery for analysing a supplied ÄŒech/Giraud H2-class, while NWW Problem 8.1(b) requires constructing and comparing that class at cochain level with the ÄŒechization of a locally smooth crossed-module H3-cocycle. The entire source-side chain model and the comparison cochain are absent.

The minimum result that would have changed my answer is a proved theorem in the restricted Lie-group category giving:


the explicit characteristic cocycle c;


the lifting-gerbe cocycle z on a common cover;


an explicit b with
Ï„(c)âˆ“z=Î´Ë‡b;


independence under all stated choices and refinements.


Nothing weakerâ€”certainly no UCT quotient or finite-nerve calculationâ€”would suffice.
Therefore: stop the NWW extension for this paper.

5. Mandatory changes before submission to Cahiers
I would require all of the following:


Correct the component-gerbe priority. Cite Giraud III.2.1.5.3 at Theorem 4.8 and reclassify the result as standard input.


Fix Proposition 5.6. Add connected nonempty intersections with coherent trivializations, or use a component nerve/local coefficient formulation.


Prove the basis-site comparison explicitly. State the sheaf-category and Leray comparison for the generated all-subdivision site.


Resolve companion overlap. Publish both manuscripts and give an overlap table, or merge them.


Move the two core proofs into the article. The representative-rigid theorem and prescribed-image construction cannot be hidden in a 50-page supplement.


Collapse elementary theorem families. In particular, reduce the UCT, aggregate quotient, and auxiliary-register material to one or two propositions.


Remove most of the team-semantic wrapper and retitle. The fixed finite-site gerbe construction, not team semantics, drives the main theorem.


Present Theorem 6.16 as a sharp realization corollary, not a new general classification.


Retain the empirical-model section as a boundary result, while compressing its standard split-stack preliminaries.


Reduce the complete referee-facing text to approximately 40 pages.


Keep the NWW issue to one sentence or delete the remark. Do not begin that extension inside this submission.


Do not invoke inert Lean markers or finite Python tests as mathematical evidence.


Items 1â€“4 are correctness and priority conditions. Items 5â€“10 are publication-level significance and presentation conditions. Item 11 is project control.
Final verdict

\title[Finite-Site Component Gerbes]{Finite-Site Component Gerbes and Homological Images}
\author{Haobo Ma}
\address[Haobo Ma]{AELF PTE LTD., \#14-02, Marina Bay Financial Centre Tower 1, 8 Marina Blvd, Singapore 018981}
\email[Haobo Ma]{auric@aelf.io}

\author{Wenlin Zhang}
\address[Wenlin Zhang]{National University of Singapore, Singapore}
\email[Wenlin Zhang]{e1327962@u.nus.edu}

\begin{document}

\begin{abstract}
For a stack, Giraud's component construction identifies the full substack
over a global component section as its maximal component gerbe.  We apply
this standard construction to a banded prestack lift of a presheaf on a
finite site.  Under slice separatedness and terminal essential surjectivity,
matching without amalgamation is equivalent to non-neutrality of every
global component gerbe.  A representative-rigidity theorem shows that a
strictly pullback-stable choice of lift representatives forces terminal
effectivity when the band has vanishing first cohomology.  On a finite good
cover with connected nonempty intersections and $H_1(N,\mathbb Z)=0$, we
give a self-contained Cech-cocycle prestack construction simultaneously
controlling the component presheaf, terminal fibre, neutral labels, and
prescribed evaluation maps $H_2(N,\mathbb Z)\to A$.  UCT and elementary
subgroup arithmetic yield the aggregate quotients.  For the selected
open-star sites of $\bigvee_{j=1}^{\beta}S^2$, a nonzero finite abelian group
$G$ occurs as the two-component comparison kernel with complementary nonzero
images exactly when $d(G)\le2\beta$ and $G$ is not cyclic of prime-power
order.  This is a realization corollary for supplied lift data and does not
transfer to bare empirical models.
\end{abstract}

\keywords{finite sites, component gerbes, banded prestacks, good covers, homological images, universal coefficient theorem}

17:The proofs of the two claims most capable of carrying originality—the representative-rigid terminal theorem and the prescribed-image prestack construction—are in the unavailable supplement. The main article itself identifies these as supplement material.  I therefore assess their statements and priority below, but I cannot certify those proofs from main.pdf.
20:I do not find evidence in the displayed statements of a false flagship finite-group equivalence. The problem is priority, significance, architecture, and one correctable coefficient hypothesis—not an obvious contradiction in the finite-abelian algebra.
22:1. Journal placement
25:RankJournalFit and likely objectionMinimum change requiredIs 48+50 acceptable?Candid likelihood1Cahiers de Topologie et Géométrie Différentielle CatégoriquesThis is the best conceptual fit: component stacks, gerbes, descent, finite nerves, and homological algebra are within its genuine subject range, not merely its vocabulary. The likely objection is that the manuscript elevates standard component-gerbe and UCT consequences into too many named theorems, while the genuinely paper-specific content is buried in a large supplement. Cahiers normally considers papers of about 40 pages and suggests dividing longer work only into somewhat independent parts. 27:Placement conclusion
40:2. Priority audit
41:Bottom-line priority judgment
42:The priority narrative is accurate about scope but not yet accurate about mathematical ancestry.
45:I did not find a previously published theorem stating the entire bespoke two-label wedge classification verbatim. That does not make it a substantial priority claim: exact statements manufactured by composing standard equivalences can be formally new while having little independent mathematical priority.
46:Claim-by-claim audit
47:2.1 Component-indexed gerbes of a stack with prescribed component sheaf
64:2.2 Matching without amalgamation versus non-neutral component gerbes
83:Smallest honest repair: call it a “presheaf-to-component-gerbe comparison criterion,” not a new obstruction classification. Give a four-line dependency diagram displaying the four facts above. State explicitly whether this paper or the companion first contained the result. If the companion predates this paper, the current theorem is a site-theoretic reformulation, not an independent priority claim.
85:2.3 Terminal-fibre rigidity with strictly pullback-stable representatives
110:Put the complete proof in the article, not only the supplement.
118:2.4 Explicit cocycle prestacks realizing prescribed H2 classes
160:2.5 Homological image and cokernel
168:2.6 Intersection/sum aggregate quotients
178:2.7 Wedge-of-spheres finite-group classification
179:The exact equivalence
205:2.8 Exact comparison with empirical models
218:The canonical split stack
224:2.9 Restricted lower-language separation
229:2.10 Companion-paper overlap
235:which paper has chronological priority;
258:Priority ledger
259:Genuinely or plausibly new
277:Standard or essentially standard
304:Already appearing in the companion project
316:Definite omitted antecedent
323:3. Mathematical and expository objections requiring correction
324:3.1 The finite-nerve Čech–simplicial comparison is missing connectedness
333:add the hypothesis that every nonempty finite intersection is connected and that the coefficient trivializations identify its section group coherently with A; or
344:3.2 The generated-site Leray comparison needs an explicit Comparison Lemma
368:If the supplement already proves exactly this for the all-subdivision site, cite the theorem verbatim in Lemma 6.14 rather than replacing it by two sentences. If it proves only the comparison for one finite intersection site, an additional passage to the common generated site is still needed.
370:3.3 The presentation-comparison groupoid currently proves too little
384:3.4 The theorem count misrepresents the amount of new mathematics
439:3.5 The team-semantic architecture is not causally connected to the classification
444:Component Gerbes and Homological Images on Finite Good-Cover Sites
448:Finite-Site Realizations of Component Gerbes and Homological Quotients
453:3.6 The “auxiliary register” results should be deleted as a theorem package
461:3.7 The 48+50-page architecture is still too large
462:The old 96-page article has been transformed into a 48-page article plus a 50-page supplement, but the mathematical referee burden remains 98 pages. More importantly, the supplement contains the rigidity and realization proofs that should determine acceptance. 
481:3.8 Machine evidence has no bearing on the decision
483:Resolution: remove any suggestion in the manuscript or cover letter that these checks support mathematical correctness. A repository note may describe them as sanity checks. Either provide actual Lean artifacts or omit formal-verification language entirely.
485:4. The NWW extension
487:The NWW comparison is not within reach of the machinery developed here.
489:NWW define the translated-cover cocycle explicitly and ask whether
493:The strict crossed-module hypothesis is mandatory
494:For
502:Exact chain-level material missing from the paper
504:1. A fixed locally smooth cochain complex
505:One needs an explicit normalized complex
509:2. An explicit characteristic 3-cocycle
518:3. NWW’s Čechization on the same cover
520:The current finite good-cover machinery does not reproduce this calculation. In particular, the NWW translated cover is generally infinite and is not one of the paper’s selected finite wedge nerves.
521:4. The lifting-gerbe cocycle on that same cover
522:One must construct local sections
531:5. The actual missing comparison lemma
536:6. Independence of choices
559:7. Naturality
561:8. Finite-presentation compatibility, if desired
591:How the sign would have to be fixed
639:Decisive stopping reason
642:The present paper has machinery for analysing a supplied Čech/Giraud H2-class, while NWW Problem 8.1(b) requires constructing and comparing that class at cochain level with the Čechization of a locally smooth crossed-module H3-cocycle. The entire source-side chain model and the comparison cochain are absent.
663:5. Mandatory changes before submission to Cahiers
667:Correct the component-gerbe priority. Cite Giraud III.2.1.5.3 at Theorem 4.8 and reclassify the result as standard input.
679:Move the two core proofs into the article. The representative-rigid theorem and prescribed-image construction cannot be hidden in a 50-page supplement.
703:Items 1–4 are correctness and priority conditions. Items 5–10 are publication-level significance and presentation conditions. Item 11 is project control.
704:Final verdict

\section{Conclusion}\label{sec:conclusion}

The article starts with supplied finite-site data: a presheaf, an
abelian-banded prestack lift, and its stackification.  Giraud's component
construction then assigns the maximal component gerbe to each global section
of the component sheaf.  The use made of this standard input is narrow: under
slice separatedness and terminal essential surjectivity it compares the
strict presheaf predicate of matching without amalgamation with non-neutrality
of every global component.  Representative-rigidity shows why a strictly
pullback-stable choice of lift representatives is too restrictive in the
basic \(H^1\)-vanishing regime.

On a finite good-cover site, the connected-overlap hypothesis is what permits
the ordinary nerve to carry the full constant-coefficient Cech complex.  The
all-subdivision basis comparison identifies the generated site with the
ordinary sheaf theory of the underlying polyhedron.  Within this fixed
setting, the standard Cech-cocycle gerbe construction can be packaged into a
single prestack whose component presheaf, terminal behavior, neutrality
pattern, and evaluation images are prescribed simultaneously.

UCT and elementary subgroup arithmetic then yield the aggregate quotients.
For the selected open-star covers of
\(\bigvee_{j=1}^{\beta}S^2\), the two-component realization criterion is
\[
 d(G)\le2\beta
 \quad\text{and}\quad
 G\text{ is not cyclic of prime-power order}.
\]
This is a sharp corollary of the selected realization, not a new
classification principle for finite abelian groups or gerbes.

Bare empirical models provide only the support presheaf and its canonical
split stack, whose component gerbes are neutral.  Their relative degree-one
obstructions are indexed differently and are not recovered by the
component-section map.  A non-split banded lift must therefore be supplied
before the finite-site results apply.

The resulting contribution has a definite boundary.  The following parts
are classical inputs:
\[
\begin{array}{ll}
\text{component over }v\text{ is a gerbe}
 &\text{Giraud III.2.1.5.3},\\
\text{neutrality}\Longleftrightarrow[\mathfrak G_v]=0
 &\text{abelian-banded gerbe classification},\\
\text{finite Cech class}\longrightarrow\text{UCT evaluation}
 &\text{Leray comparison and UCT},\\
\text{aggregate exact sequence}
 &\text{subgroup and quotient arithmetic.}
\end{array}
\]
They are stated together so that the hypotheses used by the applications
remain visible, not as separate advances.

The two proofs requiring construction beyond those inputs are terminal
rigidity and prescribed simultaneous realization.  The first identifies
where literal pullback stability removes a descent cocycle and where
\(H^1\)-vanishing removes the remaining Isom torsor.  The second constructs
the label presheaf and twisted groupoid fibres, verifies strict restrictions
and the genuine constant band, computes stackification components and
terminal objects, and recovers the selected Cech cocycles.  Their full proofs
make the claimed scope independently checkable within the article.

The technical appendix has a limited role.  Its presentation groupoid records
naturality only along specified comparison words and does not assert path
independence.  Its finite computations illustrate the UCT and subgroup
formulas.  Its lower-language example explains why the finite-site lift is
treated as input but does not construct that lift.

No crossed-module comparison is pursued: the present arguments begin after a
gerbe \(H^2\)-class and its Cech representative are supplied, and contain
neither the source-side degree-three chain model nor the comparison cochain
required to derive such a class from crossed-module data.


 succeeded in 179ms:
\section{Introduction}\label{sec:introduction}

A stack in groupoids has a sheaf of connected components.  A global section
of that sheaf need not be represented by a global object of the stack, but it
does select a maximal subgerbe.  This is Giraud's component-gerbe construction
\cite[III, Prop.~2.1.5.3]{Giraud1971}.  The component is represented by a
global object precisely when its gerbe is neutral, or equivalently when its
Giraud \(H^2\)-class vanishes.  These classical facts are the starting point
of the article.

Our setting is deliberately finite and presentation-relative.  We fix a site
with terminal object, a presheaf \(F\), an abelian-banded prestack lift
\(\mathfrak P\), and its stackification \(\mathfrak L\).  The connected
components of \(\mathfrak P\) recover \(F\), while those of
\(\mathfrak L\) recover the sheafification \(aF\).  If \(F\) is separated on
the terminal slice and stackification is essentially surjective on the
terminal fibre, a strict matching family without an amalgamation occurs
exactly when \((aF)(a)\) is nonempty and every component gerbe selected by a
section of \((aF)(a)\) is non-neutral.  This is a comparison between a strict
presheaf predicate and standard gerbe theory, not a new classification of
gerbes.

The first paper-specific point is a rigidity statement for a particularly
strong kind of prestack presentation.  Suppose one selects a representative
of every presheaf component and requires those representatives to be stable
under pullback as literal equalities with respect to a split cleavage.  Under
slice separatedness, terminal essential surjectivity then forces the
sheafification unit
\[
 F(a)\longrightarrow(aF)(a)
\]
to be surjective.  Conversely, when \(H^1\) of the band vanishes,
surjectivity makes the Isom torsor between a selected representative and an
arbitrary terminal stack object trivial.  The result is a three-way
equivalence between terminal essential surjectivity, bijectivity of the
terminal sheafification unit, and the sheaf gluing condition at the terminal
object.  The proof in \Cref{thm:canonical-lift-rigidity} distinguishes the
strict-representative step from the classical \(H^1\)-torsor step.

The second point is an explicit finite-label prestack packaging.  Let
\(\mathcal U\) be a finite good cover with connected nerve \(N\), let \(A\)
be a finite abelian group, assume \(H_1(N,\mathbb Z)=0\), and prescribe a
finite family
\[
 \phi_\lambda:H_2(N,\mathbb Z)\longrightarrow A.
\]
UCT selects classes in \(H^2(N,A)\), and the standard Cech construction
realizes each class by an \(A\)-banded gerbe
\cite{Breen2006,Moerdijk2002}.  The construction in
\Cref{thm:prescribed-component-image-construction} assembles these gerbes
into one prestack while controlling four features simultaneously:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item the presheaf and sheaf of component labels;
\item terminal essential surjectivity of stackification;
\item the exact set of neutral and non-neutral labels;
\item the evaluation maps and their image subgroups.
\end{enumerate}
The full construction and all four verifications are part of the article.

There is a coefficient hypothesis which cannot be suppressed.  For an
ordinary nerve to index the Cech cochain complex with one copy of \(A\) per
simplex, every nonempty finite overlap must be connected and the coefficient
trivializations must be coherent.  On a disconnected overlap \(U_J\), the
section group is \(A^{\pi_0(U_J)}\), information which the ordinary nerve
does not contain.  We impose the connected-overlap hypothesis explicitly in
\eqref{eq:connected-overlap-hypothesis}.  The open-star covers used for the
wedge application have contractible nonempty intersections and satisfy it.

We also separate the formal algebra from the realization.  For a class
\(\omega\in H^2(N,A)\), put
\[
 K_\omega=\operatorname{Im}
 \bigl(H_2(N,\mathbb Z)\xrightarrow{\operatorname{ev}_\omega}A\bigr),
 \qquad Q_\omega=A/K_\omega.
\]
UCT naturality, quotient initiality, character annihilators, and the Ext
kernel are collected in one proposition.  For finitely many component
classes, the two aggregate quotients are
\[
 A/\bigcap_vK_v,
 \qquad A/\sum_vK_v,
\]
and their comparison is the elementary exact sequence
\[
 0\longrightarrow
 \frac{\sum_vK_v}{\bigcap_vK_v}
 \longrightarrow
 A/\bigcap_vK_v
 \longrightarrow
 A/\sum_vK_v
 \longrightarrow0.
\]
No independent information-theoretic claim is made for the cardinalities of
these fibres.

The finite-group endpoint is correspondingly stated as a realization
corollary.  On the all-subdivision open-star basis of a finite model of
\(\bigvee_{j=1}^{\beta}S^2\), a nonzero finite abelian group \(G\) is
realized as the comparison kernel for two complementary nonzero component
images exactly when
\[
 d(G)\le2\beta
 \quad\text{and}\quad
 G\text{ is not cyclic of prime-power order}.
\]
The proof combines the prescribed-image prestack with the elementary fact
that the indecomposable finite abelian groups are the cyclic prime-power
groups.  It is not a classification attached to an arbitrary space,
presentation, or empirical model.

\subsection*{Inputs, standard steps, and paper-specific steps}

The logical order of the construction is
\[
\begin{array}{c}
(\mathcal C,F,\mathfrak P,\mathscr A)\\
\big\downarrow\text{\scriptsize stackification}\\
\mathfrak L,\ \pi_0(\mathfrak L)\cong aF\\
\big\downarrow\text{\scriptsize pull back over }v\in(aF)(a)\\
\mathfrak G_v,\ [\mathfrak G_v]\in H^2(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\\
\big\downarrow\text{\scriptsize selected finite presentation and UCT}\\
\operatorname{ev}_{\omega_v}:H_2(N,\mathbb Z)\to A,\quad
K_v,\quad Q_v.
\end{array}
\]
The first line is supplied data.  The component gerbe in the third line is
Giraud's standard maximal subgerbe over a component section.  Its neutrality
criterion and transport under a banded equivalence are also standard.  The
last line uses standard Cech comparison and UCT after a finite presentation
has been selected.

The article-specific statements concern restrictions and simultaneous
control across this sequence.  The rigidity theorem identifies a strict
representative hypothesis that collapses a possible terminal gluing failure
when \(H^1\) vanishes.  The prescribed-realization theorem constructs one
prestack in which the component presheaf, terminal fibre, neutrality set, and
finite family of UCT evaluations are fixed together.  The wedge result is
then a corollary obtained by combining that construction with finite-group
arithmetic.  It is not presented as a separate classification mechanism.

This division also explains the theorem count.  Component-gerbe existence,
neutrality, banded naturality, UCT, duality, subgroup sums and intersections,
and quotient-fibre cardinality are consolidated rather than restated as
independent theorems.  Numbered statements are retained only where they
serve one of three roles:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item record the standard component-gerbe package with its exact Giraud
      antecedent;
\item state a paper-specific comparison, rigidity, or simultaneous
      realization assertion;
\item isolate an application boundary or the wedge realization endpoint.
\end{enumerate}

There are likewise two distinct uses of cohomology.  The Giraud class
\[
 [\mathfrak G_v]\in H^2(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)
\]
decides neutrality of the full component gerbe.  After a finite connected
good-cover presentation with constant coefficient group \(A\) is fixed, its
image under
\[
 H^2(N,A)\longrightarrow
 \operatorname{Hom}(H_2(N,\mathbb Z),A)
\]
measures only evaluation on integral \(2\)-cycles.  A nonzero Ext class can
be invisible to this map.  The homological image is therefore a quotient
construction applied to a supplied gerbe class, not a complete obstruction
theory for neutrality.

\subsection*{Scope and relation to the companion manuscript}

The lift data in this article are supplied.  They are not constructed from
teams, logical models, or empirical probability tables.  A bare empirical
model has a canonical split stack lift whose component gerbes are neutral.
The Abramsky--Mansfield--Barbosa obstruction is instead a relative Cech
\(H^1\)-torsor class indexed by a local section
\cite{AbramskyMansfieldSoaresBarbosa2012,Caru2017}.  The empty-domain argument
in \Cref{thm:empirical-model-boundary} prevents a universal recovery of those
classes from a global-component-section map.

The companion manuscript
\cite{MaZhang2026ConditionalGluingFailure} uses meet-semilattice sites.  The
overlap with the present pullback-site treatment is as follows.
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.31\textwidth}
                >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.27\textwidth}
                >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.30\textwidth}}
\textbf{Topic here} & \textbf{Companion location} & \textbf{Status here}\\
\hline
Matching versus non-neutral components & Theorem 5.20 & Pullback-site
reformulation with the dependency chain displayed\\
Quotient initiality & Theorem 6.13 & Standard finite-nerve UCT clause in
\Cref{prop:uct-aggregate-package}\\
Component obstruction framework & Section 5 & Standard Giraud construction,
cited at theorem level\\
Terminal representative rigidity & No identical statement & Full narrow
no-go proof in \Cref{thm:canonical-lift-rigidity}\\
Finite-label prescribed realization & No identical package & Simultaneous
control theorem in \Cref{thm:prescribed-component-image-construction}\\
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The table identifies overlap rather than claiming separate priority for the
first three rows.

\subsection*{Organization}

Section~\ref{sec:preliminaries} fixes the minimal presheaf and prestack
interface.  Section~\ref{sec:gerbe-obstruction} records the standard Giraud
component package, proves the comparison criterion, and gives the full
terminal-rigidity proof.  Section~\ref{sec:homological-visibility} proves the
connected-overlap, basis-site, and derived comparisons before constructing
the prescribed finite-label prestack.  Section~\ref{sec:homological-images}
collects the UCT and aggregate consequences.  The wedge application and the
empirical-model boundary occupy Sections~\ref{sec:wedge-application} and
\ref{sec:empirical-boundary}.  The separately compiled technical appendix
contains only presentation bookkeeping, extended examples, and the narrow
lower-language boundary argument; the proofs needed to certify the principal
claims are all contained here.

\begin{document}

\begin{abstract}
For a stack, Giraud's component construction identifies the full substack
over a global component section as its maximal component gerbe.  We apply
this standard construction to a banded prestack lift of a presheaf on a
finite site.  Under slice separatedness and terminal essential surjectivity,
matching without amalgamation is equivalent to non-neutrality of every
global component gerbe.  A representative-rigidity theorem shows that a
strictly pullback-stable choice of lift representatives forces terminal
effectivity when the band has vanishing first cohomology.  On a finite good
cover with connected nonempty intersections and $H_1(N,\mathbb Z)=0$, we
give a self-contained Cech-cocycle prestack construction simultaneously
controlling the component presheaf, terminal fibre, neutral labels, and
prescribed evaluation maps $H_2(N,\mathbb Z)\to A$.  UCT and elementary
subgroup arithmetic yield the aggregate quotients.  For the selected
open-star sites of $\bigvee_{j=1}^{\beta}S^2$, a nonzero finite abelian group
$G$ occurs as the two-component comparison kernel with complementary nonzero
images exactly when $d(G)\le2\beta$ and $G$ is not cyclic of prime-power
order.  This is a realization corollary for supplied lift data and does not
transfer to bare empirical models.
\end{abstract}

\keywords{finite sites, component gerbes, banded prestacks, good covers, homological images, universal coefficient theorem}
\subjclass[2020]{18F20, 18G50, 55N30}
