\end{equation}
which is exactly the simplicial coboundary of \(N\).  If an overlap is
empty, its section group is zero and there is no corresponding simplex.
This gives the cochain-level, rather than merely cohomological,
identification used by the realization theorem.

\begin{proposition}[Connected-overlap and basis-site comparison]
\label{prop:finite-site-comparison}
Let \(A\) be an abelian group.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item For a finite cover satisfying
      \eqref{eq:connected-overlap-hypothesis}, the ordered Cech complex of
      \(A_{\mathcal U}\) is naturally the simplicial cochain complex of the
      ordinary nerve:
      \[
      \check C^q(\mathcal U,A_{\mathcal U})
      \cong C^q(N,A).
      \]
\item If the nonempty intersections are also
      \(A_{\mathcal U}\)-acyclic, the Leray comparison gives
      \[
      H^q(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U},A_{\mathcal U})
      \cong \check H^q(\mathcal U,A_{\mathcal U})
      \cong H^q(N,A).
      \]
\item Let \(K\) be a finite simplicial complex, put \(X=|K|\), and let
      \(\mathcal U^{(m)}\) be the open-star cover of
      \(\operatorname{Sd}^mK\).  The category \(\mathcal B_K\) of all open
      stars in these covers and all their finite intersections is a basis
      for \(X\), stable under finite intersections after adjoining the empty
      set.  The topology generated on \(\mathcal B_K\) is the induced
      open-cover topology, and restriction gives an equivalence
      \[
      \mathbf {Sh}(X)\simeq\mathbf {Sh}(\mathcal B_K).
      \]
      Under this equivalence the genuine constant sheaf on
      \(\mathcal B_K\) is the restriction of the ordinary constant sheaf on
      \(X\).  Every nonempty basic intersection is contractible, the covers
      \((\mathcal U^{(m)})_{m\ge0}\) are filtered and cofinal, and
      \[
      \varinjlim_m\check H^2(\mathcal U^{(m)},A)
      \cong H^2(X,A).
      \]
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For (i), an ordered Cech \(q\)-cochain assigns a section of
\(A_{\mathcal U}\) to each nonempty ordered \((q+1)\)-fold intersection.
Connectedness and the coherent trivializations identify that section group
with one copy of \(A\), and the restriction maps with the identity of
\(A\).  Empty intersections have section group zero.  The ordered Cech
coboundary is therefore exactly the alternating simplicial coboundary
\eqref{eq:cech-simplicial-differential}.  The identification commutes with
restriction to a subcover and with every refinement map compatible with the
chosen coefficient trivializations.

For (ii), the Cech-to-derived spectral sequence for \(\mathcal U\) has
\[
 E_1^{p,q}=\prod_{i_0<\cdots<i_p}
 H^q(U_{i_0\cdots i_p},A_{\mathcal U}).
\]
Acyclicity makes every row with \(q>0\) vanish, so the edge map is an
isomorphism.  Clause (i) identifies its remaining row with the nerve
cochain complex; this is the usual Leray theorem
\cite[Chap.~III, Sec.~4]{Bredon1997}.

For the formal intersection site, the same vanishing is visible directly in
\eqref{eq:formal-slice-acyclicity}.  The augmented Cech resolution
\[
 0\longrightarrow A_{\mathcal U}
 \longrightarrow
 \prod_i(j_i)_*A_{\mathcal U}|_{U_i}
 \longrightarrow
 \prod_{i<j}(j_{ij})_*A_{\mathcal U}|_{U_{ij}}
 \longrightarrow\cdots
\]
has acyclic terms because every nonempty intersection slice has exact global
sections.  Applying global sections computes derived cohomology by the same
ordered complex.  This makes clear that the comparison is a property of the
specified site and band, not of an abstract nerve alone.

For (iii), open stars in successive barycentric subdivisions have arbitrarily
small mesh.  Given \(x\in O\) with \(O\subseteq X\) open, a sufficiently
fine subdivision has a vertex whose open star contains \(x\) and lies in
\(O\).  Hence the union of all these stars is a basis.  Adding their finite
intersections makes it stable under intersections, and a family of basis
objects covers precisely when their union covers in \(X\).  Thus its
generated topology is the topology induced from the open-set site.

To make the small-mesh assertion explicit, fix a metric on the compact
polyhedron \(X\).  Barycentric subdivision reduces the mesh of each simplex
by a uniform factor strictly less than one.  Choose
\(\varepsilon>0\) with \(B(x,\varepsilon)\subseteq O\).  At a sufficiently
late subdivision, every simplex meeting \(x\) has diameter less than
\(\varepsilon/2\).  A vertex belonging to a smallest simplex whose closure
contains \(x\) then has open star contained in \(O\).  Applying this
argument to every member of a finite open cover and using compactness shows
that a sufficiently late star cover refines that cover.

The Comparison Lemma for a basis stable under intersections now gives the
displayed equivalence of sheaf categories; explicitly, restriction is fully
faithful because sections are recovered by gluing over basis covers, and its
quasi-inverse sends a basis sheaf to the sheaf whose sections on an open set
are compatible basis-local sections.  This also shows directly that locally
constant \(A\)-valued functions restrict to the genuine constant sheaf used
on \(\mathcal B_K\) \cite[Chap.~III, Sec.~7]{MacLaneMoerdijk1994}.

A nonempty intersection of open stars is the open star of the simplex spanned
by the corresponding vertices, hence is contractible.  Barycentric
subdivision refines the preceding cover, and every finite basis cover is
refined by a sufficiently late subdivision cover; the sequence is therefore
filtered and cofinal.  The basic intersections are acyclic for the constant
sheaf, so (ii), the sheaf-category equivalence, and cofinality give
\[
 \varinjlim_m\check H^2(\mathcal U^{(m)},A)
 \cong H^2(\mathcal B_K,A)
 \cong H^2(X,A).
\]
This proves every asserted comparison, including the passage from the
all-subdivision generated site to the usual topological site.

The order of the argument is essential.  Contractibility supplies
connectedness and acyclicity on each selected cover.  The basis comparison
identifies sheaves on the generated site with sheaves on \(X\).  Cofinality
then identifies the colimit over subdivision covers with the full Cech
colimit.  Only after these three checks is the final derived-cohomology
identification justified.
\end{proof}

We turn to the paper-specific packaging of the standard Cech-cocycle gerbe
construction.  The point is not that a class in \(H^2\) can be represented
by a gerbe; that is classical.  The content is simultaneous control of the
component presheaf, terminal essential surjectivity, neutral and non-neutral
labels, and the images of the evaluation homomorphisms.

\begin{theorem}[Finite-site realization of prescribed componentwise evaluation maps]

 succeeded in 183ms:
\end{proof}

We turn to the paper-specific packaging of the standard Cech-cocycle gerbe
construction.  The point is not that a class in \(H^2\) can be represented
by a gerbe; that is classical.  The content is simultaneous control of the
component presheaf, terminal essential surjectivity, neutral and non-neutral
labels, and the images of the evaluation homomorphisms.

\begin{theorem}[Finite-site realization of prescribed componentwise evaluation maps]
\label{thm:prescribed-component-image-construction}
Let \(\mathcal U=\{U_i\}_{i\in I}\) be a finite good cover satisfying
\eqref{eq:connected-overlap-hypothesis}, with connected nerve \(N\), and let
\(A\) be a finite abelian group.  Assume
\[
 H_1(N,\mathbb Z)=0.
\]
Let \(\Lambda\) be a finite nonempty set and prescribe homomorphisms
\[
 \phi_\lambda:H_2(N,\mathbb Z)\longrightarrow A
 \qquad(\lambda\in\Lambda).
\]
Put \(\Lambda_0=\{\lambda:\phi_\lambda=0\}\).  Then there are a presheaf
\(F\) on \(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}\) and an
\(A_{\mathcal U}\)-banded prestack lift
\(\mathfrak P\), with stackification \(\mathfrak L\), such that:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item \(F(a)=\Lambda_0\), \((aF)(a)\cong\Lambda\), and
      \(\pi_0(\mathfrak L)(a)\cong\Lambda\);
\item the component gerbe \(\mathfrak G_\lambda\) has class
      \(\omega_\lambda\in H^2(N,A)\) satisfying
      \[
      \operatorname{ev}_{\omega_\lambda}=\phi_\lambda,
      \qquad
      \operatorname{Im}(\operatorname{ev}_{\omega_\lambda})
      =\operatorname{Im}(\phi_\lambda),
      \]
      and \(\mathfrak G_\lambda\) is neutral exactly when
      \(\lambda\in\Lambda_0\);
\item \(\mathfrak P(a)\to\mathfrak L(a)\) is essentially surjective, and
      its terminal objects occur exactly in the components indexed by
      \(\Lambda_0\);
\item \(F\) has a strict matching family on \(\mathcal U\), and
      \[
      \operatorname{MWA}(F,a)
      \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
      \Lambda_0=\varnothing.
      \]
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The universal coefficient sequence and \(H_1(N,\mathbb Z)=0\) give a natural
isomorphism
\begin{equation}\label{eq:uct-realization-isomorphism}
 \eta_A:H^2(N,A)\xrightarrow{\sim}
 \operatorname{Hom}(H_2(N,\mathbb Z),A).
\end{equation}
For every \(\lambda\), let \(\omega_\lambda\) be the inverse image of
\(\phi_\lambda\).  Choose a normalized simplicial cocycle
\(\alpha_\lambda\in Z^2(N,A)\) representing it, choosing
\(\alpha_\lambda=0\) when \(\lambda\in\Lambda_0\).  Extend it alternately
to ordered triples and set it to zero on triples with a repeated index.

The choice \(\alpha_\lambda=0\) for a zero map is legitimate because
\(\eta_A\) is injective: \(\phi_\lambda=0\) implies
\(\omega_\lambda=0\).  Conversely, a nonzero prescribed map has a nonzero
class.  For pairwise distinct indices, alternating extension means
\[
 \alpha_{\lambda,\sigma(i)\sigma(j)\sigma(k)}
 =\operatorname{sgn}(\sigma)\alpha_{\lambda,ijk}.
\]
On every ordered \(3\)-simplex, the cocycle equation is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:realization-cocycle-equation}
 \alpha_{\lambda,jkl}-\alpha_{\lambda,ikl}
 +\alpha_{\lambda,ijl}-\alpha_{\lambda,ijk}=0.
\end{equation}
These are the only coefficient identities used in the groupoid
construction.

Define the component presheaf by
\[
 F(W)=
 \begin{cases}
 \Lambda,&W\ne a,\varnothing\text{ is a nonempty intersection object},\\
 \Lambda_0,&W=a,\\
 \{*\},&W=\varnothing.
 \end{cases}
\]
Restrictions among proper nonempty intersections are identities,
restrictions from \(a\) are the inclusion
\(\Lambda_0\hookrightarrow\Lambda\), and restrictions to
\(\varnothing\) are unique.  A matching family on the vertex cover has a
single label on every connected component of the nerve.  Since \(N\) is
connected, its label is a unique element of \(\Lambda\).  Consequently
\begin{equation}\label{eq:label-sheafification}
 (aF)(a)\cong\Lambda.
\end{equation}

Here is the sheafification calculation in detail.  A family
\((\lambda_i)_{i\in I}\) with \(\lambda_i\in F(U_i)=\Lambda\) is matching
exactly when
\[
 \lambda_i=\lambda_j
 \quad\text{for every edge }\{i,j\}\in N.
\]
No condition is imposed by an empty overlap because both restrictions land
in the singleton \(F(\varnothing)\).  Edgewise equality propagates along
paths in the connected \(1\)-skeleton of \(N\), so every matching family is
constant.  Conversely, each constant family is matching.  It determines a
section of \(aF\) over \(a\), and distinct constants remain distinct after
restriction because every nonempty cover member retains its label.

Every cover in the generated pretopology is obtained from the vertex cover
by pullback and transitivity.  On a nonempty proper intersection, a generated
cover contains an identity, so it neither creates nor identifies labels.
It follows that the vertex-cover calculation computes all terminal sections
of the sheafification.  On proper nonempty objects,
\((aF)(W)=\Lambda\), and on the initial object it is a singleton.

We next construct the prestack and verify its structure rather than citing a
separate realization result.  Fix \(\lambda\notin\Lambda_0\).  Put
\(\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)=\varnothing\).  For a nonempty proper intersection
\(W\), let
\[
 I(W)=\{i\in I:W\subseteq U_i\}.
\]
The groupoid \(\mathfrak P_\lambda(W)\) has objects
\(x_i^{\lambda,W}\) for \(i\in I(W)\), and
\[
 \operatorname{Hom}(x_i^{\lambda,W},x_j^{\lambda,W})
 =A_{\mathcal U}(W)\cong A.
\]
Writing a morphism as \((b,i,j)\), define
\begin{equation}\label{eq:twisted-composition}
 (c,j,k)\circ(b,i,j)
 =(b+c+\alpha_{\lambda,ijk},i,k).
\end{equation}
Normalization supplies identities and inverses.  Explicitly,
\[
 1_{x_i^{\lambda,W}}=(0,i,i),
 \qquad
 (b,i,j)^{-1}=(-b,j,i),
\]
because every cocycle coordinate with a repeated index is zero.  For three
composable arrows, the two parenthesizations are
\[
\begin{aligned}
 (d,k,l)\circ\bigl((c,j,k)\circ(b,i,j)\bigr)
 &=(b+c+d+\alpha_{\lambda,ijk}
       +\alpha_{\lambda,ikl},i,l),\\
 \bigl((d,k,l)\circ(c,j,k)\bigr)\circ(b,i,j)
 &=(b+c+d+\alpha_{\lambda,jkl}
       +\alpha_{\lambda,ijl},i,l).
\end{aligned}
\]
They agree by \eqref{eq:realization-cocycle-equation}.  Associativity is
therefore exactly the cocycle identity
\[
 \alpha_{\lambda,ijk}+\alpha_{\lambda,ikl}
 =\alpha_{\lambda,jkl}+\alpha_{\lambda,ijl}.
\]
If \(V\subseteq W\), then \(I(W)\subseteq I(V)\); restriction keeps the
object index and applies the sheaf restriction to the coefficient.  These
functors compose strictly.  Over \(\varnothing\) take the terminal groupoid.

To verify the restriction assertion, write
\(\rho_{VW}:A_{\mathcal U}(W)\to A_{\mathcal U}(V)\).  The functor sends
\[
 x_i^{\lambda,W}\longmapsto x_i^{\lambda,V},
 \qquad
 (b,i,j)\longmapsto(\rho_{VW}(b),i,j).
\]
On nonempty connected intersections, the coherent trivializations identify
\(\rho_{VW}\) with the identity of \(A\).  Hence
\[
 \rho_{VW}(b+c+\alpha_{\lambda,ijk})
 =\rho_{VW}(b)+\rho_{VW}(c)+\alpha_{\lambda,ijk},
\]
so restriction preserves \eqref{eq:twisted-composition}.  If
\(Z\subseteq V\subseteq W\), the equality
\(\rho_{ZV}\rho_{VW}=\rho_{ZW}\) gives literal equality of the restriction
functors.  This is strict functoriality, not functoriality only up to a
cartesian comparison arrow.

For fixed \(i,j\in I(W)\), the presheaf of arrows between the corresponding
objects on the slice over \(W\) is
\[
 V\longmapsto A_{\mathcal U}(V).
\]
It is the restriction of the genuine coefficient sheaf, and hence is a
sheaf.  This verifies morphism descent for every twisted summand.
Translation in the coefficient coordinate identifies
\[
 \underline{\operatorname{Aut}}(x_i^{\lambda,W})
 \cong A_{\mathcal U}|_{\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}/W}.
\]
Changing \(i\) transports this identification through an arrow
\((b,i,j)\); abelianness makes the induced conjugation on \(A\) trivial.
The identifications therefore constitute an \(A_{\mathcal U}\)-banding.

For \(\lambda\in\Lambda_0\), let \(\mathfrak P_\lambda\) be the neutral
one-object \(A_{\mathcal U}\)-banded stack on every nonempty object and the
terminal groupoid on \(\varnothing\).  This is a stack here, not merely a
one-object prestack: on the slice over a nonempty formal intersection, every
generated cover contains an identity, so the nonempty part of the topology
is trivial; the empty cover forces value zero at \(\varnothing\).  Evaluation
at the terminal slice object is exact, whence
\(H^1(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}/W,A_{\mathcal U})=0\), and every object
descent torsor is trivial.

Concretely, descent data for the distinguished local object on a cover
\(\{V_r\to W\}\) are arrows represented by coefficients
\(d_{rs}\in A_{\mathcal U}(V_{rs})\) satisfying
\[
 d_{rs}+d_{st}=d_{rt}.
\]
They form an \(A_{\mathcal U}\)-torsor cocycle.  The vanishing just noted
provides coefficients \(e_r\in A_{\mathcal U}(V_r)\) with
\(d_{rs}=e_s-e_r\).  Adjusting the local identifications by \(e_r\) makes
them agree and yields the global object.  Morphism descent is the ordinary
sheaf condition for \(A_{\mathcal U}\).  Thus the neutral summand is already
a stack on every relevant slice.

Set
\[
 \mathfrak P=\coprod_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\mathfrak P_\lambda
\]
fibrewise on nonempty objects, with a shared terminal fibre over
\(\varnothing\).  For two objects in a twisted fibre, the isomorphism
presheaf on the relevant slice is the restriction of the genuine sheaf
\(A_{\mathcal U}\); it is therefore a sheaf.  Automorphism sheaves are the
same sheaf, so \(\mathfrak P\) is an \(A_{\mathcal U}\)-banded prestack.
Every nonempty proper fibre has connected components indexed by \(\Lambda\),
the terminal fibre has components \(\Lambda_0\), and the empty fibre has one
component.  Thus
\[
 \pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P)\cong F.
\]
Stackification and \eqref{eq:label-sheafification} prove (i).

There are two points in this coproduct verification.  First, over a nonempty
object there are no arrows between different \(\lambda\)-summands, so
isomorphism descent cannot change a label.  A matching object descent datum
therefore lies in one summand on each connected part of a cover, and the
connected nerve forces a single label.  Second, all summands restrict to the
same terminal groupoid over \(\varnothing\).  This causes no identification
on a nonempty overlap: the initial object has no arrow back to a nonempty
object and its unique descent datum contributes no coefficient.

For a twisted summand, every two named objects are isomorphic, since their
Hom set is the nonempty set \(A\).  Thus each such fibre is connected.
The neutral summand has one object and is connected as well.  It follows
objectwise that the component presheaf is exactly \(F\), including its
restriction \(\Lambda_0\hookrightarrow\Lambda\) out of the terminal object.
The standard compatibility of stackification with components gives
\[
 \pi_0(\mathfrak L)\cong aF,
\]
and the terminal component sections are the labels in \(\Lambda\).

On \(U_i\), choose \(x_i^{\lambda,U_i}\); on \(U_{ij}\), choose the arrow
\((0,i,j)\).  By \eqref{eq:twisted-composition}, its triple-overlap defect is
\[
 (0,i,k)^{-1}(0,j,k)(0,i,j)
 =\alpha_{\lambda,ijk}
\]
under the band identification.  The Cech representative of the component
gerbe is therefore exactly \(\alpha_\lambda\).  The good-cover comparison
in \Cref{prop:finite-site-comparison} identifies its Giraud class with
\(\omega_\lambda\), and
\eqref{eq:uct-realization-isomorphism} gives
\(\operatorname{ev}_{\omega_\lambda}=\phi_\lambda\).  By the standard
component-gerbe package, this gerbe is neutral exactly when its class is zero,
equivalently when \(\phi_\lambda=0\).  This proves (ii).

To check the sign in the defect, use
\[
 (0,i,k)^{-1}=(0,k,i)
\]
and apply \eqref{eq:twisted-composition} twice.  Normalization cancels the
terms with repeated indices, while alternating extension moves the remaining
term to the ordered coordinate \(\alpha_{\lambda,ijk}\).  This is the same
convention as \eqref{eq:giraud-triple-defect}.  Therefore the class obtained
by stackification is not merely cohomologous to a class with the desired
evaluation: its chosen vertex-cover representative is the initially
selected cocycle \(\alpha_\lambda\).

The connected-overlap hypothesis enters here a second time.  It identifies
the automorphism section on \(U_{ijk}\) with one element of \(A\), so the
defect is an ordinary simplicial coordinate.  If \(U_{ijk}\) were
disconnected, the same calculation would yield a tuple in
\(A^{\pi_0(U_{ijk})}\), and the prescribed ordinary-nerve cocycle would not
specify the entire defect.

For \(\lambda\in\Lambda_0\), the neutral summand is already a stack and its
terminal object maps to the corresponding terminal object of
\(\mathfrak L\).  If \(\lambda\notin\Lambda_0\), the component gerbe is
non-neutral and therefore has no object over \(a\).  Hence every object of
\(\mathfrak L(a)\) lies in a zero component and comes from
\(\mathfrak P(a)\), proving (iii), including the vacuous case
\(\Lambda_0=\varnothing\).

When \(\Lambda_0\ne\varnothing\), an object of
\(\mathfrak L(a)\) has some component label
\(\lambda\in\Lambda\).  The component-gerbe package identifies the full
substack at that label with \(\mathfrak G_\lambda\).  If
\(\lambda\notin\Lambda_0\), non-neutrality says that this full substack has
no terminal object, so the assumed object cannot have that label.  If
\(\lambda\in\Lambda_0\), the distinguished terminal object of the neutral
summand of \(\mathfrak P\) maps to an object in the same component of
\(\mathfrak L\).  Any other terminal object in that component is isomorphic
to it because the neutral component is a gerbe.  This is precisely essential
surjectivity, rather than equality of object sets.

Finally, assigning one fixed \(\lambda\in\Lambda\) to every \(U_i\) is a
strict matching family: restrictions on nonempty overlaps are identities and
those on empty overlaps are unique.  It has an amalgamation precisely when
\(F(a)=\Lambda_0\) is nonempty.  This proves (iv) and completes the
construction.

In terms of the definition from \Cref{sec:preliminaries}, the last
equivalence uses only existence.  If \(\Lambda_0=\varnothing\), every
constant label gives a strict matching family and there is no global
section, so matching without amalgamation holds.  If
\(\Lambda_0\ne\varnothing\), \(F(a)\) is nonempty and the predicate
\(\operatorname{MWA}(F,a)\) is false, although matching families with
nonzero labels may still fail individually to amalgamate.  The theorem does
not claim that every matching family amalgamates in this case.
\end{proof}

The gerbes in this proof are obtained from the standard Cech-cocycle
construction.  What the theorem adds is the single finite-label prestack
whose sheaf of components, terminal fibre, neutrality pattern, and homological
images are all prescribed simultaneously.

\subsection*{The two terminal-fibre endpoints}

\section{Homological images and aggregate quotients}
\label{sec:homological-images}

Fix a finite simplicial complex \(N\), a finite abelian group \(A\), and a
class \(\omega\in H^2(N,A)\).  The universal coefficient map is denoted by
\[
 \operatorname{ev}_\omega:
 H_2(N,\mathbb Z)\longrightarrow A.
\]
Define its \emph{homological image} and \emph{homological cokernel} by
\begin{equation}\label{eq:homological-image-cokernel}
 K_\omega:=\operatorname{Im}(\operatorname{ev}_\omega),
 \qquad Q_\omega:=A/K_\omega.
\end{equation}
These definitions depend on the supplied finite presentation of the component
gerbe.  Refinement and retrivialization transport them by UCT naturality, but
no canonical identification along distinct comparison zigzags is asserted.
The resulting freely generated presentation groupoid and its elementary
naturality calculation are recorded in the technical appendix rather than
promoted to an invariance theorem.

For a finite family of component classes
\((\omega_v)_{v\in V}\), put \(K_v=K_{\omega_v}\) and define
\begin{equation}\label{eq:aggregate-quotients}
 Q_{\mathrm{lab}}:=A\Big/\bigcap_{v\in V}K_v,
 \qquad
 Q_{\mathrm{com}}:=A\Big/\sum_{v\in V}K_v.
\end{equation}
The first quotient retains enough information to map to every componentwise
quotient \(A/K_v\).  The second retains only characters which descend through
every one of them.

\begin{proposition}[UCT and aggregate subgroup package]
\label{prop:uct-aggregate-package}
With the notation above:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item The universal coefficient sequence is
      \[
      0\longrightarrow
      \operatorname{Ext}^1(H_1(N,\mathbb Z),A)
      \longrightarrow H^2(N,A)
      \xrightarrow{\operatorname{ev}}
      \operatorname{Hom}(H_2(N,\mathbb Z),A)
      \longrightarrow0.
      \]
      Thus \(K_\omega=0\) exactly when \(\omega\) lies in the displayed
      Ext subgroup.  In particular, nonzero classes can have zero
      homological image when \(H_1\) has torsion.
\item The quotient map \(q_\omega:A\to Q_\omega\) is initial among group
      homomorphisms \(q:A\to B\) satisfying
      \(q\circ\operatorname{ev}_\omega=0\).  A character
      \(\chi:A\to\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z\) factors through \(Q_\omega\)
      exactly when \(\chi(K_\omega)=0\).  Since finite Pontryagin duality
      separates points,
      \[
      K_\omega=\mathop{\bigcap}_{\chi(K_\omega)=0}\ker\chi.
      \]
\item For a nonempty finite family \((K_v)_{v\in V}\), there is a natural
      short exact sequence
      \begin{equation}\label{eq:aggregate-exact-sequence}
      0\longrightarrow
      \frac{\sum_vK_v}{\bigcap_vK_v}
      \longrightarrow Q_{\mathrm{lab}}
      \longrightarrow Q_{\mathrm{com}}
      \longrightarrow0.
      \end{equation}
      Moreover,
      \[
      \widehat{Q_{\mathrm{com}}}
      \cong\bigcap_{v\in V}K_v^\perp,
      \qquad
      \widehat{Q_{\mathrm{lab}}}
      \cong\sum_{v\in V}K_v^\perp
      \]
      inside \(\widehat A\).
\item If \(H_1(N,\mathbb Z)=0\) and
      \(H_2(N,\mathbb Z)\cong\mathbb Z^\beta\), a subgroup
      \(K\le A\) occurs as \(K_\omega\) exactly when \(d(K)\le\beta\).
      Every finite family of such subgroups is simultaneously attained by
      the prestack of
      \Cref{thm:prescribed-component-image-construction}.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Clause (i) is the cohomological universal coefficient theorem, natural in
both \(N\) and \(A\) \cite[Sec.~3.1]{Hatcher2002}.  Its middle-to-right map
evaluates a cohomology class on integral \(2\)-cycles, so its value at
\(\omega\) is \(\operatorname{ev}_\omega\).  Exactness identifies the
kernel with the Ext term.

For a simplicial map \(f:N'\to N\), naturality is the commutative square
\[
\begin{CD}
H^2(N,A) @>{\operatorname{ev}}>>
 \operatorname{Hom}(H_2(N,\mathbb Z),A)\\
@V{f^*}VV @VV{(-)\circ f_*}V\\
H^2(N',A) @>{\operatorname{ev}}>>
 \operatorname{Hom}(H_2(N',\mathbb Z),A).
\end{CD}
\]
Thus
\[
 \operatorname{ev}_{f^*\omega}
 =\operatorname{ev}_\omega\circ f_*.
\]
For a coefficient homomorphism \(\theta:A\to A'\), the corresponding
identity is
\[
 \operatorname{ev}_{\theta_*\omega}
 =\theta\circ\operatorname{ev}_\omega.
\]
These formulas are the only invariance input used for a specified
presentation comparison.  The short exact UCT sequence may split as a
sequence of groups, but no natural splitting is chosen.

For (ii), \(q\circ\operatorname{ev}_\omega=0\) says precisely that \(q\)
vanishes on \(K_\omega\).  The universal property of the quotient gives a
unique factorization through \(A/K_\omega\).  Apply the same observation to
characters.  The intersection formula follows because characters of a
finite abelian group separate points \cite{Terras1999}.

For the last assertion, pass to the finite quotient \(A/K_\omega\).  If
\(a\notin K_\omega\), then \(\bar a\ne0\) in that quotient.  Finite
Pontryagin duality gives a character
\[
 \bar\chi:A/K_\omega\longrightarrow\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z
\]
with \(\bar\chi(\bar a)\ne0\).  Its pullback \(\chi=\bar\chi q_\omega\)
annihilates \(K_\omega\) but not \(a\).  Hence no element outside
\(K_\omega\) belongs to the displayed intersection of kernels.  The reverse
containment is immediate.

The containment \(\bigcap_vK_v\subseteq\sum_vK_v\) gives the quotient map in
\eqref{eq:aggregate-exact-sequence}.  By the third isomorphism theorem its
kernel is
\[
 \frac{\sum_vK_v}{\bigcap_vK_v},
\]
which proves exactness.  For finite abelian groups, annihilators exchange sums and
intersections:
\[
 \Big(\sum_vK_v\Big)^\perp=\bigcap_vK_v^\perp,
 \qquad
 \Big(\bigcap_vK_v\Big)^\perp=\sum_vK_v^\perp.
\]
Dualizing the two quotients proves the character formulas in (iii).

The first annihilator identity does not require finiteness:
\(\chi\) kills \(\sum_vK_v\) exactly when it kills every \(K_v\).  For the
second, one inclusion is immediate:
\[
 \sum_vK_v^\perp\subseteq\Big(\bigcap_vK_v\Big)^\perp.
\]
For finite groups, equality follows from exactness of Pontryagin duality.
It may also be checked by cardinalities, using
\[
 |H^\perp|=\frac{|A|}{|H|}
\]
and, for two subgroups,
\[
 |K+L|\,|K\cap L|=|K|\,|L|.
\]
Induction gives the formula for a finite family.

Under the hypotheses of (iv), the Ext term vanishes and UCT becomes
\[
 H^2(N,A)\cong\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb Z^\beta,A).
\]
The image of a homomorphism from \(\mathbb Z^\beta\) is generated by the
images of the standard basis, so it has at most \(\beta\) generators.
Conversely, given generators \(k_1,\dots,k_\beta\) of \(K\), allowing zeros
when fewer are needed, the map
\[
 (n_1,\dots,n_\beta)\longmapsto\sum_jn_jk_j
\]
has image \(K\).  Applying
\Cref{thm:prescribed-component-image-construction} to a family of these maps
gives simultaneous realization.

The number \(d(K)\) can be read prime by prime.  If
\[
 K\cong\bigoplus_p\bigoplus_{j=1}^{r_p}C_{p^{e_{p,j}}},
\]
then
\[
 d(K)=\max_p r_p.
\]
Thus the condition in (iv) is not a bound on \(|K|\) or on its exponent.
It is precisely the rank bound imposed by the \(\beta\) basis elements of
\(H_2(N,\mathbb Z)\).  Once homomorphisms with the desired images have been
chosen componentwise, simultaneous attainment is the prestack-level part
supplied by \Cref{thm:prescribed-component-image-construction}.
\end{proof}

\subsection*{How to read the aggregate quotients}

There are canonical maps
\[
 Q_{\mathrm{lab}}\longrightarrow A/K_v
 \qquad(v\in V),
\]
because \(\bigcap_wK_w\subseteq K_v\).  The product of these maps is
injective:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:labelled-product-embedding}
 A/\bigcap_vK_v

 succeeded in 182ms:
\section{The finite-site interface}\label{sec:preliminaries}

The results use a site, a presheaf, and a banded prestack as input.  No
semantic construction of these data is assumed.  This section records the
small interface needed later and fixes the distinction between a matching
family and an amalgamation.

Let \(\mathcal C\) be a small site with pullbacks of covering families and
terminal object \(a\), and let
\[
 F:\mathcal C^{\mathrm{op}}\longrightarrow\mathbf {Set}
\]
be a presheaf.  If \(\mathcal U=\{U_i\to a\}_{i\in I}\) is a cover, write
\(U_{ij}=U_i\times_aU_j\).  A \emph{strict matching family} on
\(\mathcal U\) is a family \(s_i\in F(U_i)\) satisfying
\[
 s_i|_{U_{ij}}=s_j|_{U_{ij}}
 \qquad(i,j\in I).
\]
It is an \emph{amalgamation} if there is \(s\in F(a)\) with
\(s|_{U_i}=s_i\) for every \(i\).  We use the abbreviations
\[
 \operatorname{Match}(F,a),\qquad
 \operatorname{Glob}(F,a),\qquad
 \operatorname{MWA}(F,a)
\]
for, respectively, existence of a strict matching family on some cover of
\(a\), nonemptiness of \(F(a)\), and
\[
 \operatorname{Match}(F,a)\ \text{and}\ F(a)=\varnothing.
\]
Thus \(\operatorname{MWA}\) means matching without amalgamation.  It is not
a claim about every matching family on every cover.

\subsection*{Three terminal levels}

Three sets occur at the terminal object and should not be identified without
hypotheses:
\[
 F(a),\qquad (aF)(a),\qquad
 \pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P)(a).
\]
The chosen lift identifies the first and third sets, whereas sheafification
maps the first to the second.  A member of \(F(a)\) is a strict global
presheaf section.  A member of \((aF)(a)\) is represented locally and may
have no strict representative in \(F(a)\).  A component section of the
stackification is again an element of \((aF)(a)\), but it need not be the
component of an actual object of \(\mathfrak L(a)\): the full stack over
that component can be a non-neutral gerbe.

This gives the sequence
\begin{equation}\label{eq:three-terminal-levels}
 \operatorname{Ob}(\mathfrak P(a))
 \longrightarrow
 \operatorname{Ob}(\mathfrak L(a))
 \longrightarrow
 \pi_0(\mathfrak L)(a)
 \cong(aF)(a).
\end{equation}
The first map is stackification and the second takes a fibrewise isomorphism
class.  The composite on components is the sheafification unit
\[
 u_a:F(a)\longrightarrow(aF)(a).
\]
The last arrow in \eqref{eq:three-terminal-levels} need not be surjective:
a global section of a component sheaf is only a locally inhabited
component, while an object of \(\mathfrak L(a)\) is a global object in that
component.  Giraud's construction measures exactly this difference.

Terminal essential surjectivity concerns objects, not component sections.
It says that every object already present in \(\mathfrak L(a)\) comes from
\(\mathfrak P(a)\) up to isomorphism.  It does not say that every member of
\((aF)(a)\) is represented by an object of \(\mathfrak L(a)\).  The latter
assertion would force every global component gerbe to be neutral.

Write \(u:F\to aF\) for the sheafification unit.  We say that \(F\) is
\emph{separated on the slice over \(a\)} if, for every \(X\to a\), two
sections of \(F(X)\) which agree after restriction to a cover of \(X\) are
equal.  This stronger slice condition, rather than separatedness only at
\(a\), is used whenever equality in \(aF\) is returned to equality in
\(F\).

The slice qualifier is needed because a plus--plus representative of a
terminal sheaf section first gives equality only after a cover of an overlap.
That overlap is an object \(U_{ij}\to a\), and the equality must be returned
from \(aF(U_{ij})\) to \(F(U_{ij})\).  Separatedness only of
\(F(a)\to(aF)(a)\) does not provide this step.

Let \(\mathscr A\) be an abelian sheaf on \(\mathcal C\).  An
\emph{\(\mathscr A\)-banded prestack lift of \(F\)} is a prestack in
groupoids \(\mathfrak P\) with a fixed split cleavage, together with
compatible identifications
\[
 \pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P)\cong F,
 \qquad
 \underline{\operatorname{Aut}}_{\mathfrak P}(x)\cong\mathscr A
\]
on every slice on which \(x\) is defined.  Let
\[
 \iota:\mathfrak P\longrightarrow\mathfrak L:=a(\mathfrak P)
\]
be stackification.  Standard compatibility of stackification with connected
components gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:stackification-components-core}
 \pi_0(\mathfrak L)\cong aF,
\end{equation}
and the map induced by \(\iota\) on components is the sheafification unit.
We say that \(\iota\) is \emph{terminally essentially surjective} if
\(\mathfrak P(a)\to\mathfrak L(a)\) is essentially surjective.

The lift is \emph{representative-rigid} if there are maps
\[
 s_X:F(X)\longrightarrow\operatorname{Ob}(\mathfrak P(X))
\]
selecting a representative of every component and satisfying, for every
\(f:Y\to X\),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:strict-representative-pullback}
 f^*s_X(t)=s_Y(f^*t)
\end{equation}
as a literal equality.  The equality is taken with respect to the fixed
split cleavage.  Ordinary choices of cartesian lifts are generally only
pseudofunctorial; the strictness in
\eqref{eq:strict-representative-pullback} is therefore a substantive extra
hypothesis, not a harmless choice of notation.  Split cleavages and their
relation to pseudofunctorial pullback are standard for fibred categories
\cite[Chap.~VI]{MacLaneMoerdijk1994}.

A componentwise representative choice alone would instead provide objects
\(s_X(t)\) and, for \(f:Y\to X\), some isomorphism
\[
 f^*s_X(t)\xrightarrow{\sim}s_Y(f^*t).
\]
On a composable pair \(Z\xrightarrow{g}Y\xrightarrow{f}X\), the two induced
isomorphisms from \((fg)^*s_X(t)\) to \(s_Z((fg)^*t)\) need not agree.
Their ratio is an automorphism, hence a section of the band.  On triple
overlaps these ratios can contribute a nonzero \(2\)-cocycle.  The equality
\eqref{eq:strict-representative-pullback} eliminates the comparison arrows
and their defect.  The rigidity theorem uses exactly this elimination and
does not claim that an arbitrary fibred category admits it.

\begin{proposition}[Presheaf and prestack interface]
\label{prop:finite-site-semantic-interface}
With the notation above:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item a strict matching family determines a section of \((aF)(a)\); if
      \(F\) is separated on the slice over \(a\), every section of
      \((aF)(a)\) is represented by a strict matching family on a cover of
      \(a\).  Hence, under this separatedness hypothesis,
      \[
      \operatorname{Match}(F,a)
      \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
      (aF)(a)\ne\varnothing;
      \]
\item \(\mathfrak P(a)\ne\varnothing\) if and only if \(F(a)\ne\varnothing\).
      If \(\iota\) is terminally essentially surjective, these are also
      equivalent to \(\mathfrak L(a)\ne\varnothing\);
\item under separatedness on the slice and terminal essential surjectivity,
      \(\operatorname{MWA}(F,a)\) holds exactly when \((aF)(a)\) is
      nonempty and \(\mathfrak L(a)\) is empty.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Applying \(u\) to a strict matching family gives a matching family in the
sheaf \(aF\), which has a unique amalgamation in \((aF)(a)\).  Conversely,
let \(\xi\in(aF)(a)\).  The plus--plus description of sheafification gives
a cover \(\{U_i\to a\}\) and sections \(s_i\in F(U_i)\) representing
\(\xi|_{U_i}\), with the following local equality on each overlap: there is
a cover \(\{V_{ijk}\to U_{ij}\}_k\) such that
\[
 s_i|_{V_{ijk}}=s_j|_{V_{ijk}}.
\]
Every \(U_{ij}\to a\) is an object of the slice.  Separatedness there turns
these locally witnessed equalities into
\(s_i|_{U_{ij}}=s_j|_{U_{ij}}\).  The \(s_i\) therefore form a strict
matching family.  This proves (i).

The first plus construction identifies matching families after passage to a
common covering refinement.  The second plus construction turns the
resulting separated presheaf into a sheaf.  A representative of \(\xi\) may
therefore begin with sections whose restrictions agree only locally on
\(U_{ij}\).  Pulling the witnessing covers back makes the local equalities
simultaneous.  Slice separatedness then shows that the original two
restrictions were already equal in \(F(U_{ij})\).  This is why the conclusion
produces a strict matching family on one cover rather than only a family
compatible after unrelated refinements.

The isomorphism \(\pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P)(a)\cong F(a)\) proves
the first equivalence in (ii).  The forward implication from
\(\mathfrak P(a)\ne\varnothing\) to \(\mathfrak L(a)\ne\varnothing\) is
given by \(\iota\), and the converse is terminal essential surjectivity.
Finally, (iii) is obtained by combining (i), (ii), and the definition of
\(\operatorname{MWA}(F,a)\).
\end{proof}

The proposition can be read as a dependency audit:
\[
\begin{array}{c|c}
\text{comparison}&\text{hypothesis used}\\ \hline
\operatorname{Match}(F,a)\Longleftrightarrow(aF)(a)\ne\varnothing
 &\text{slice separatedness}\\
\mathfrak P(a)\ne\varnothing\Longleftrightarrow F(a)\ne\varnothing
 &\pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P)\cong F\\
\mathfrak L(a)\ne\varnothing\Longleftrightarrow\mathfrak P(a)\ne\varnothing
 &\text{terminal essential surjectivity.}
\end{array}
\]
No band, cohomology group, or gerbe classification is needed for these
set-level comparisons.  Those enter only after a global component section
has been fixed.

# Literature and Priority Check

Date of record: 15 August 2026 (Asia/Singapore).

## Scope and evidentiary basis

This record states the priority boundary supported by the current manuscript,
its `references.bib`, and the external assessment in
`oracle_sprint_A9_referee.md`. It is not a claim that an exhaustive literature
search can certify absolute priority. In particular, a statement not found
verbatim in the referee's targeted search is not thereby a substantial new
mathematical result.

All bibliographic metadata asserted below were checked against
`references.bib`. Chapter and proposition locators were also checked against
the citations in the manuscript. No unconfirmed bibliographic detail is used.

## Direct antecedent: Giraud's component-gerbe construction

The relevant book is Jean Giraud, *Cohomologie non abelienne*, Grundlehren der
mathematischen Wissenschaften 179, Springer-Verlag, 1971. These metadata are
the metadata recorded in `references.bib`.

Chapter III, Proposition 2.1.5.3 is the direct antecedent for the
component-gerbe construction. For any stack `S`, the projection

```text
S -> pi_0(S)
```

makes `S` a gerbe over its sheaf of connected components, and pullback along a
section of `pi_0(S)` gives the corresponding maximal subgerbe. As the referee
observes, this is essentially the structural content of Theorems 4.8(i) and
4.9. It is an input to this paper, not a contribution of this paper. The map
that assigns the gerbe class of the component selected by a section is useful
packaging for later comparisons, but it is not a new construction principle.

The paper also cites Giraud, Chapter IV for the classification of
abelian-banded gerbes by `H^2`, including the identification of neutral gerbes
with the zero class. That classification is a different result from the
Chapter III component-gerbe construction. Both results are used here, and
both are prior work.

## Standard inputs and formal consequences

The referee's assessment is that much of the advertised theorem structure is
textbook gerbe theory, universal-coefficient-theorem naturality, finite
duality, subgroup arithmetic, or fibre counting. The exact organization may
be specific to this manuscript, but the mathematical advance is smaller than
the theorem count and theorem-level presentation suggest.

The following boundaries therefore apply.

| Material used in the paper | Priority status |
|---|---|
| A component selected by a global component section is a maximal subgerbe | Giraud, Chapter III, Proposition 2.1.5.3; standard input |
| Neutrality is equivalent to existence of a global object, and abelian-banded gerbes are classified by `H^2` | Giraud, Chapter IV; standard input |
| Local objects, overlap arrows, triple-overlap Cech 2-cocycles, and change of representative by a coboundary | Standard gerbe theory |
| Realization of a supplied `H^2` class by an abelian-band gerbe | Standard gerbe theory |
| The matching/non-neutrality equivalence | A paper-specific corollary obtained by matching separatedness, sheafification, terminal essential surjectivity, and neutrality; not a new gerbe obstruction theorem, and substantially present in the cited companion project |
| Homological images, Ext-kernel descriptions, annihilating-character descriptions, and quotient factorization | Formal consequences of UCT naturality, finite duality, and quotient universal properties |
| Intersection/sum quotients and their exact sequence | Elementary subgroup and quotient arithmetic |
| Unrestricted auxiliary-register sizes | Fibre cardinality of quotient maps, not an independent information-theoretic result |
| The finite-abelian decomposition used in the wedge result | Standard finite-abelian group structure and generator arithmetic |
| The split component stack used in the empirical-model comparison | Standard construction |

Accordingly, whenever this manuscript applies one of these facts in its chosen
finite-site notation, that application should be described as an application
of a standard result. New notation, a named quotient, or a theorem-sized
restatement does not by itself establish mathematical priority.

## Two-label wedge classification

The referee found no previously published theorem stating the entire
two-label wedge classification verbatim. That negative search result must be
recorded together with its qualification: it does not support a substantial
priority claim. An exact statement obtained by composing standard
equivalences can be formally new while carrying little independent
mathematical priority.

Here the wedge statement is derived from `H_2` of a wedge of 2-spheres, the
description of attainable images of maps from a free abelian group, the
equivalence between complementary image subgroups and an internal direct-sum
decomposition, the classification of finite indecomposable abelian groups,
and generator bounds on the two summands. The resulting exact criterion may
be specific to this selected realization problem, but it is an elementary
two-component realization corollary, not a new classification theorem about
finite abelian groups or gerbes. It is also presentation-relative to the
chosen good-cover construction.

## Closed route: Neeb-Wagemann-Wockel comparison

The bibliography records Karl-Hermann Neeb, Friedrich Wagemann, and Christoph
Wockel, *Making Lifting Obstructions Explicit*, Proceedings of the London
Mathematical Society 106(3) (2013), 589--620, DOI
`10.1112/plms/pds047`, arXiv:`1108.5853`.

The crossed-module comparison discussed in connection with that work is an
explicitly closed route for this paper. This paper begins only after a gerbe
`H^2` class and a Cech representative of that class have been supplied. It
does not contain the source-side degree-three locally continuous or locally
smooth group-cohomology chain model, the characteristic 3-cocycle derived
from strict crossed-module data, or the comparison cochain needed to identify
its Cechization with the lifting-gerbe 2-cocycle.

In particular, the missing step is not supplied by the paper's UCT images,
finite quotients, banded-equivalence naturality, or wedge calculation. It
would require an explicit cochain comparison, with conventions, choice
independence, refinement compatibility, and naturality proved at chain level.
The current paper therefore neither derives a gerbe class from crossed-module
data nor claims to solve that comparison problem. Pursuing the comparison
would require a separate source-side theory and a separate proof, not an
extension of the results recorded here.

## Defensible paper-specific remainder

After the prior inputs and formal consequences above are removed from the
priority account, the defensible paper-specific content is narrow:

1. The exact representative-rigid terminal-fibre no-go formulation is the
   strongest plausible technical priority claim identified by the referee.
   Its formulation may be new, but its ingredients - split cleavages,
   pseudofunctorial pullback choices, and `H^1` classification of torsors -
   are classical. It is not a new general descent principle.

2. The exact prestack presentation simultaneously controls the component
   presheaf, terminal essential surjectivity, neutral versus non-neutral
   labels, and prescribed componentwise homological images, and places these
   controls inside the paper's typed-model convention. This simultaneous
   finite-label/presheaf packaging is paper-specific. The underlying
   Cech-cocycle gerbe realization and the disjoint-union assembly are standard
   inputs.

3. The exact two-label wedge criterion is, at most, a sharp algebraic
   corollary for the selected realization construction. The referee found no
   verbatim antecedent, but this supports only a claim about the exact
   packaging, not a substantial independent classification priority.

4. The empty-domain comparison with local-section-indexed contextuality
   classes is a useful narrow boundary observation: for a strongly contextual
   empirical model there is no global component section, while the comparison
   classes are indexed by local sections. The split stack used to expose this
   mismatch is standard.

5. The one-model, one-variable, parameter-free, constant-free lower-language
   separation example is plausibly specific to the paper but deliberately
   narrow. It does not establish general expressive incomparability.

The organization of presentation-relative homological images is bookkeeping
that may be useful to readers, not an independent priority claim. The
referee also identified unresolved overlap with an unpublished companion
manuscript for the component-obstruction framework, the matching/non-neutral
criterion, and quotient initiality. Until that overlap is documented in a
theorem-by-theorem public comparison, chronological priority for those parts
cannot be certified here.

This is the priority boundary for the submission package: standard inputs are
credited as standard, exact packaging is not inflated into a broad novelty
claim, and the remaining claims are limited to the concrete formulations and
simultaneous controls listed above.

+I am asking for a tier-raising mathematical assessment of the attached current manuscript, main.pdf. This is not a journal-placement request. We already know where the paper lands at its present strength. I want you to identify a single named, concrete, reachable new theorem that would materially raise the paper's mathematical tier, or to explain rigorously why no such theorem is reachable from its present machinery.

+Here is the paper's setup and its principal results, stated with their hypotheses.
+
+Let C be a small site with pullbacks of covering families and terminal object a, let F be a presheaf of sets, and let P be a prestack in groupoids with a fixed split cleavage, banded by an abelian sheaf A-script, such that the presheaf of fibrewise connected components of P is F. Write L = a(P) for stackification and aF for sheafification. A strict matching family is a family of local sections equal on all fibre-product overlaps. MWA(F,a), "matching without amalgamation", means that such a matching family exists on some cover of a while F(a) is empty; it does not mean that every matching family fails to amalgamate. "Separated on the slice over a" means separated on every object X -> a. "Terminal essential surjectivity" means that P(a) -> L(a) is essentially surjective. For v in (aF)(a) = pi_0(L)(a), let G_v be the full component substack L[v].
+
+1. Presheaf/component-gerbe comparison. If F is separated on the slice over a and P is an abelian-banded prestack lift whose stackification is terminally essentially surjective, then
+
+   MWA(F,a) iff (aF)(a) is nonempty and every G_v, v in (aF)(a), is non-neutral,
+
+equivalently, every Giraud class omega_v = [G_v] in H^2(C,A-script) is nonzero. The proof factors through four facts: slice separatedness identifies existence of a strict matching family with nonemptiness of (aF)(a); Giraud's component construction makes L[v] a gerbe; terminal essential surjectivity identifies nonemptiness of F(a) with nonemptiness of L(a); and a component has a terminal global object exactly when its gerbe is neutral.
+
+2. Representative-rigid terminal theorem. Call the lift representative-rigid if one can choose an object s_X(t) in P(X) in every component t in F(X) such that f^*s_X(t) = s_Y(f^*t) as a literal equality for every f:Y -> X, relative to the fixed split cleavage. If F is separated on the slice over a and P is a representative-rigid A-script-banded prestack lift, with sheafification unit u:F -> aF and stackification map iota:P -> L, then:
+
+   (i) terminal essential surjectivity of iota implies surjectivity of u_a:F(a) -> (aF)(a);
+
+   (ii) if H^1(C,A-script)=0, surjectivity of u_a implies terminal essential surjectivity of iota;
+
+   (iii) under H^1(C,A-script)=0, the following are equivalent: iota is terminally essentially surjective; u_a is bijective; and every strict matching family on every cover of a has a unique amalgamation in F(a).
+
+Literal pullback stability is used only in the forward descent step; the converse uses the usual A-script-torsor of isomorphisms and its H^1-class.
+
+3. Finite-cover comparison used by the realization theorem. Let U be a finite cover with ordinary nerve N. Assume every nonempty finite intersection U_J is connected and that coherent trivializations of the locally constant coefficient sheaf identify Gamma(U_J,A-script) with the same abelian group A. Then the ordered Cech complex is naturally C^*(N,A). If the nonempty intersections are also A-script-acyclic, Leray comparison gives H^q(C_U,A_U) = Cech H^q(U,A_U) = H^q(N,A). For a finite simplicial complex K, the site B_K made from all open stars in all barycentric subdivisions of K and their finite intersections is a basis site for |K|, restriction gives Sh(|K|) equivalent to Sh(B_K), the nonempty basic intersections are contractible, the subdivision star covers are filtered and cofinal, and colim_m Cech H^2(U^(m),A) = H^2(|K|,A).
+
+4. Prescribed simultaneous realization. Let U be a finite good cover satisfying the connected-overlap/coherent-trivialization hypothesis, let its nerve N be connected, let A be a finite abelian group, and assume H_1(N,Z)=0. Given a finite nonempty label set Lambda and arbitrary homomorphisms
+
+   phi_lambda:H_2(N,Z) -> A  (lambda in Lambda),
+
+put Lambda_0 = {lambda : phi_lambda=0}. The paper explicitly constructs a presheaf F on the finite intersection site C_U and an A_U-banded prestack P, with stackification L, such that:
+
+   (i) F(a)=Lambda_0, (aF)(a)=Lambda, and pi_0(L)(a)=Lambda;
+
+   (ii) the component gerbe G_lambda has a class omega_lambda in H^2(N,A) with evaluation map ev_(omega_lambda)=phi_lambda and image Im(phi_lambda), and G_lambda is neutral exactly for lambda in Lambda_0;
+
+   (iii) P(a) -> L(a) is essentially surjective, and terminal objects occur exactly in the Lambda_0-components;
+
+   (iv) F has a strict matching family on U, and MWA(F,a) holds exactly when Lambda_0 is empty.
+
+The construction chooses the UCT class, a normalized simplicial 2-cocycle, twisted groupoid fibres with that cocycle in their composition law, and a fibrewise coproduct over the labels. Its claimed contribution is simultaneous control of the component presheaf, terminal fibre, neutrality pattern, and evaluation images, not realization of an H^2-class by a gerbe by itself.
+
+5. Homological images and aggregate quotients. For a finite simplicial complex N, finite abelian A, and omega in H^2(N,A), define
+
+   K_omega = Im(ev_omega:H_2(N,Z) -> A),    Q_omega = A/K_omega.
+
+For a nonempty finite family (omega_v), define
+
+   Q_lab = A/(intersection_v K_v),    Q_com = A/(sum_v K_v).
+
+The paper records the UCT exact sequence
+
+   0 -> Ext^1(H_1(N,Z),A) -> H^2(N,A) -> Hom(H_2(N,Z),A) -> 0,
+
+so K_omega=0 exactly for an Ext-class; the initiality of A -> Q_omega among coefficient maps killing ev_omega; the annihilating-character description of K_omega; and the exact sequence
+
+   0 -> (sum_v K_v)/(intersection_v K_v) -> Q_lab -> Q_com -> 0,
+
+with the corresponding finite Pontryagin-dual formulas. If H_1(N,Z)=0 and H_2(N,Z)=Z^beta, a subgroup K <= A occurs as K_omega exactly when its minimal number of generators d(K) is at most beta, and every finite family of such subgroups is attained simultaneously by the preceding prestack construction.
+
+6. Sharp two-component wedge realization. Fix beta >= 1 and a finite connected simplicial complex K_beta with |K_beta| homotopy equivalent to the wedge of beta copies of S^2. On the selected all-subdivision open-star basis site B_beta, let G be a finite abelian group. The following are equivalent:
+
+   (i) G is nonzero, d(G) <= 2 beta, and G is not cyclic of prime-power order;
+
+   (ii) on one selected finite open-star cover, the prescribed-realization prestack has two non-neutral component gerbes with nonzero homological images K_1,K_2 satisfying K_1 intersection K_2 = 0 and K_1 + K_2 = G.
+
+In that case Q_lab is isomorphic to G, Q_com=0, the kernel of Q_lab -> Q_com is G, and the constructed component presheaf has matching without amalgamation. This is explicitly presentation-relative and is not claimed to classify all gerbes, arbitrary presentations, or finite abelian groups independently of the constructed realization.
+
+7. Boundary with standard empirical models. For a finite empirical model e on a finite measurement set with finite nonempty outcome sets and a finite antichain of contexts covering the measurements, the terminally augmented support presheaf has
+
+   (aS_e)(X) = compatible supported families = globally supported assignments = global sections in the Abramsky-Brandenburger sense.
+
+Thus strong contextuality is absence of a matching family at this set-valued level, not MWA. For every supplied abelian sheaf A-script, Disc(aS_e) x B A-script is a canonical split banded stack lift and all its component gerbes are neutral. The Abramsky-Mansfield-Barbosa obstruction attached to a local section is instead a relative Cech H^1 torsor class; it cannot universally be recovered from a global-component-section map with component sheaf aS_e, because for a strongly contextual model that map has empty domain while local-section-indexed AMB classes remain. The finite-site homological images and wedge criterion therefore apply to an empirical model only after a band and a non-split prestack lift are supplied as extra data; they are not invariants of the bare empirical model.
+
+The imported-versus-original boundary is important here and should be read against our interest. Imported or essentially standard material includes: Giraud III.2.1.5.3, which says that a stack over its component sheaf is a gerbe and that pullback along a component section is the corresponding maximal subgerbe; Giraud's abelian-banded H^2 classification and neutrality criterion; local-object/overlap-arrow/triple-defect Cech cocycles and their change by coboundaries; realization of H^2-classes by abelian-banded gerbes; Leray and ordinary-nerve comparisons under the stated hypotheses; UCT naturality and its Ext kernel; finite Pontryagin duality; quotient universal properties; subgroup sum/intersection arithmetic and fibre counting; the structure theorem for finite abelian groups; and the standard split stack used at the empirical-model boundary.
+
+Our defensible paper-specific remainder is narrower: the exact representative-rigid terminal no-go formulation; the one explicit finite-label prestack that simultaneously controls the component presheaf, terminal essential surjectivity, neutral/non-neutral labels, and prescribed homological images; the exact negative empty-domain comparison with local-section-indexed AMB classes; and, at most, the exact two-label wedge realization criterion as a sharp corollary of that selected construction. The matching/non-neutrality criterion is substantially present in our cited companion manuscript as a meet-semilattice analogue, and quotient initiality also appears there; this paper gives a pullback-site reformulation and does not claim separate priority for those facts. The component-obstruction framework likewise overlaps the companion. The narrow lower-language separation is only in the technical appendix and is not being offered as a general logic theorem.
+
+The current priority audit also records the following adverse facts. Giraud III.2.1.5.3 is a direct theorem-level antecedent, not merely background. No prior theorem was found that states the bespoke two-label wedge equivalence verbatim, but that negative search does not establish substantial priority: the statement is composed from standard equivalences and elementary finite-abelian arithmetic. The homological images are invariants only along specified presentation comparisons; the manuscript does not prove path independence across arbitrary refinement zigzags. Bare empirical models do not canonically produce the non-split lifts on which the positive results depend. Neeb-Wagemann-Wockel Problem 8.1(b), concerning an explicit comparison between a crossed-module characteristic H^3-class and a lifting-gerbe Cech H^2-class, was identified in the earlier assessment as a named open direction, but also as out of reach here: this paper begins after the gerbe class and Cech representative are supplied and lacks the source-side locally smooth degree-three complex, explicit characteristic cocycle, comparison cochain, choice independence, refinement compatibility, and naturality. The earlier referee said that solving it would be a separate paper, not a plausible extension of this one.
+
+Machine evidence is limited and must not be overread. The repository has a five-test Python standard-library suite whose archived run says "Ran 5 tests" and "OK". Its finite batteries report 6,561 group-cocycle cases, 6,561 Cechization-rewrite cases, 59,049 Cech-cocycle cases, 2,935 finite quotient-factorization cases, 3,896 exact-sequence cases, seven sample finite groups and 160 generator-classification cases, a pullback-direction example, and a counterexample showing that an action lift need not satisfy the Peiffer identity. The archive expressly leaves NWW Problems 8.1(b), 8.2(a), and 8.2(b) unverified. There is no actual Lean formalization: the project audit records 0 of 13 claims with Lean counterparts, and earlier inert "leanverified" markers are not evidence. These numerical and finite batteries are regression tests and sanity checks only. They are never substitutes for proof and provide no evidence for the general theorems; the mathematical evidence must be the proofs in main.pdf.
+
+Your previous referee verdict on the earlier 48-page article plus 50-page supplement was: "reject and invite a substantially reframed resubmission elsewhere." You emphasized that this was not a finding of an obvious contradiction in the flagship finite-abelian equivalence. Your stated problems were instead priority, significance, architecture, and a correctable coefficient hypothesis. In particular, you found the priority account unsafe because the component-gerbe construction had the direct Giraud antecedent above; judged much of the theorem structure to be textbook gerbe theory, UCT naturality, finite duality, subgroup arithmetic, or fibre counting, with a mathematical advance smaller than the theorem count suggested; found a missing connected-overlap/coherent-coefficient hypothesis in the general finite-nerve Cech-simplicial comparison; and declined to certify the two most plausibly original results because their proofs were then relegated to an unavailable supplement. You also judged that the team-semantic/contextuality architecture was not causally generative for the classification, that the paper's higher semantic layers were supplied typed expansions rather than existence or closure theorems for lower-layer models, and that exact statements mechanically composed from standard equivalences can be formally new while carrying little independent mathematical priority. You said APAL should not receive that version because only a materially stronger logic theorem could repair the mismatch; more generally, you regarded the paper as lacking the significance needed for the higher categorical or homotopy tier. You suggested that such a tier would require genuinely new mathematics such as coherent/path-independent transport over a natural category of presentations, a functorial realization theorem with nonconstant bands/local systems, or a homotopy-natural theorem over a meaningful class of spaces. You also said the NWW comparison was not reachable from the manuscript and should be stopped as an extension of this paper.
+
+The attached current main.pdf is the 34-page reframing produced in response to that report: it now cites and labels Giraud's component theorem as standard input; imposes connected nonempty overlaps and coherent coefficient trivializations; proves the basis-site comparison; puts the complete rigidity and prescribed-realization proofs in the article; consolidates the UCT and subgroup consequences; states the wedge result as a selected realization corollary; sharply limits the empirical-model claim; includes an explicit companion-overlap table; and does not pursue NWW. Please do not reward these repairs merely for complying with the prior report: the question now is whether a further mathematical result can raise the ceiling you identified.
+
+Please answer the following in this order.
+
+1. Name the single most valuable NEW RESULT that would move this paper up one genuine mathematical tier. State it as a precise theorem, with objects, hypotheses, conclusion, and the exact sense in which it is invariant, functorial, sharp, or attached to a standard object. Do not answer with a topic, programme, or phrase such as "add local coefficients". If there are several credible candidates, state each as a theorem and rank them, but still identify one first choice.
+
+2. For each candidate, say whether it is reachable from the machinery already in this paper. Identify exactly which existing propositions, constructions, cocycles, comparisons, or estimates in main.pdf would feed the proof; then identify the genuinely new missing ingredient. If the candidate is not reachable from what is here, say so plainly and specify the replacement machinery that would be required. Distinguish a hard extension of the present proof from a new research programme wearing the same vocabulary.
+
+3. Rank the candidates by success probability multiplied by tier impact. Give a numerical probability of proving each candidate correctly within one focused follow-up project, not a verbal probability. Also give a numerical tier-impact score on a common 0-10 scale and display the resulting product, explaining the main failure mode behind each probability.
+
+4. Decide which of these four standard tier-raising levers genuinely applies here, and state exactly how:
+
+   (a) settling a named open problem in the literature;
+
+   (b) proving something about the field's own standard objects rather than about objects defined by this paper;
+
+   (c) removing a hypothesis from an existing theorem, especially one currently assumed for convenience;
+
+   (d) proving sharpness or a matching bound showing that an existing estimate cannot be improved.
+
+For every lever you select, give the precise target theorem and the literature or existing theorem against which it is measured. For every lever you reject, explain why it would not raise the tier here. In particular, test whether removing H_1(N,Z)=0, connected-overlap/constant-band assumptions, finite-site restrictions, representative rigidity, or presentation dependence would yield a genuinely stronger theorem rather than merely a more technical restatement.
+
+5. Even if the first-ranked new result is proved and integrated cleanly, what is the strongest remaining objection a referee at the higher tier would raise? State the objection in its hardest form and say what evidence or theorem would resolve it.
+
+Constraints on your answer:
+
+- Do not answer the tier question by recommending a lower-tier journal. We already know where the paper lands as it stands. We are asking what would move it up.
+
+- Do not propose merely adding more theorems of the kind already present. An earlier round of ours mechanically accumulated results and moved nothing; six of our seven papers were then found to have priority omissions, because formally new statements composed from standard equivalences carry little independent priority. More UCT clauses, quotient identities, finite examples, named definitions, or variants of the same supplied-lift realization do not answer this question.
+
+- Do not use the machine checks as support for a general theorem. They are sanity checks only, never substitutes for proof.
+
+- If the honest answer is that no reachable result exists and this paper cannot rise, say that plainly and explain which structural feature caps it: for example, dependence on author-supplied lifts, presentation relativity, lack of a standard-object application, or the fact that the strongest conclusions are formal consequences of classical equivalences. A clear, well-argued ceiling will be acted on; vague encouragement will not.
+
+A hard, specific negative is more useful to us than a soft positive. Attach to every objection the precise theorem, construction, comparison, or evidence that would resolve it.

+I am asking for a tier-raising mathematical assessment of the attached current manuscript, main.pdf. This is not a journal-placement request. We already know where the paper lands at its present strength. I want you to identify a single named, concrete, reachable new theorem that would materially raise the paper's mathematical tier, or to explain rigorously why no such theorem is reachable from its present machinery.
+
+Here is the paper's setup and its principal results, stated with their hypotheses.
+
+Let C be a small site with pullbacks of covering families and terminal object a, let F be a presheaf of sets, and let P be a prestack in groupoids with a fixed split cleavage, banded by an abelian sheaf A-script, such that the presheaf of fibrewise connected components of P is F. Write L = a(P) for stackification and aF for sheafification. A strict matching family is a family of local sections equal on all fibre-product overlaps. MWA(F,a), "matching without amalgamation", means that such a matching family exists on some cover of a while F(a) is empty; it does not mean that every matching family fails to amalgamate. "Separated on the slice over a" means separated on every object X -> a. "Terminal essential surjectivity" means that P(a) -> L(a) is essentially surjective. For v in (aF)(a) = pi_0(L)(a), let G_v be the full component substack L[v].
+
+1. Presheaf/component-gerbe comparison. If F is separated on the slice over a and P is an abelian-banded prestack lift whose stackification is terminally essentially surjective, then
+
+   MWA(F,a) iff (aF)(a) is nonempty and every G_v, v in (aF)(a), is non-neutral,
+
+equivalently, every Giraud class omega_v = [G_v] in H^2(C,A-script) is nonzero. The proof factors through four facts: slice separatedness identifies existence of a strict matching family with nonemptiness of (aF)(a); Giraud's component construction makes L[v] a gerbe; terminal essential surjectivity identifies nonemptiness of F(a) with nonemptiness of L(a); and a component has a terminal global object exactly when its gerbe is neutral.
+
+2. Representative-rigid terminal theorem. Call the lift representative-rigid if one can choose an object s_X(t) in P(X) in every component t in F(X) such that f^*s_X(t) = s_Y(f^*t) as a literal equality for every f:Y -> X, relative to the fixed split cleavage. If F is separated on the slice over a and P is a representative-rigid A-script-banded prestack lift, with sheafification unit u:F -> aF and stackification map iota:P -> L, then:
+
+   (i) terminal essential surjectivity of iota implies surjectivity of u_a:F(a) -> (aF)(a);
+
+   (ii) if H^1(C,A-script)=0, surjectivity of u_a implies terminal essential surjectivity of iota;
+
+   (iii) under H^1(C,A-script)=0, the following are equivalent: iota is terminally essentially surjective; u_a is bijective; and every strict matching family on every cover of a has a unique amalgamation in F(a).
+
+Literal pullback stability is used only in the forward descent step; the converse uses the usual A-script-torsor of isomorphisms and its H^1-class.
+
+3. Finite-cover comparison used by the realization theorem. Let U be a finite cover with ordinary nerve N. Assume every nonempty finite intersection U_J is connected and that coherent trivializations of the locally constant coefficient sheaf identify Gamma(U_J,A-script) with the same abelian group A. Then the ordered Cech complex is naturally C^*(N,A). If the nonempty intersections are also A-script-acyclic, Leray comparison gives H^q(C_U,A_U) = Cech H^q(U,A_U) = H^q(N,A). For a finite simplicial complex K, the site B_K made from all open stars in all barycentric subdivisions of K and their finite intersections is a basis site for |K|, restriction gives Sh(|K|) equivalent to Sh(B_K), the nonempty basic intersections are contractible, the subdivision star covers are filtered and cofinal, and colim_m Cech H^2(U^(m),A) = H^2(|K|,A).
+
+4. Prescribed simultaneous realization. Let U be a finite good cover satisfying the connected-overlap/coherent-trivialization hypothesis, let its nerve N be connected, let A be a finite abelian group, and assume H_1(N,Z)=0. Given a finite nonempty label set Lambda and arbitrary homomorphisms
+
+   phi_lambda:H_2(N,Z) -> A  (lambda in Lambda),
+
+put Lambda_0 = {lambda : phi_lambda=0}. The paper explicitly constructs a presheaf F on the finite intersection site C_U and an A_U-banded prestack P, with stackification L, such that:
+
+   (i) F(a)=Lambda_0, (aF)(a)=Lambda, and pi_0(L)(a)=Lambda;
+
+   (ii) the component gerbe G_lambda has a class omega_lambda in H^2(N,A) with evaluation map ev_(omega_lambda)=phi_lambda and image Im(phi_lambda), and G_lambda is neutral exactly for lambda in Lambda_0;
+
+   (iii) P(a) -> L(a) is essentially surjective, and terminal objects occur exactly in the Lambda_0-components;
+
+   (iv) F has a strict matching family on U, and MWA(F,a) holds exactly when Lambda_0 is empty.
+
+The construction chooses the UCT class, a normalized simplicial 2-cocycle, twisted groupoid fibres with that cocycle in their composition law, and a fibrewise coproduct over the labels. Its claimed contribution is simultaneous control of the component presheaf, terminal fibre, neutrality pattern, and evaluation images, not realization of an H^2-class by a gerbe by itself.
+
+5. Homological images and aggregate quotients. For a finite simplicial complex N, finite abelian A, and omega in H^2(N,A), define
+
+   K_omega = Im(ev_omega:H_2(N,Z) -> A),    Q_omega = A/K_omega.
+
+For a nonempty finite family (omega_v), define
+
+   Q_lab = A/(intersection_v K_v),    Q_com = A/(sum_v K_v).
+
+The paper records the UCT exact sequence
+
+   0 -> Ext^1(H_1(N,Z),A) -> H^2(N,A) -> Hom(H_2(N,Z),A) -> 0,
+
+so K_omega=0 exactly for an Ext-class; the initiality of A -> Q_omega among coefficient maps killing ev_omega; the annihilating-character description of K_omega; and the exact sequence
+
+   0 -> (sum_v K_v)/(intersection_v K_v) -> Q_lab -> Q_com -> 0,
+
+with the corresponding finite Pontryagin-dual formulas. If H_1(N,Z)=0 and H_2(N,Z)=Z^beta, a subgroup K <= A occurs as K_omega exactly when its minimal number of generators d(K) is at most beta, and every finite family of such subgroups is attained simultaneously by the preceding prestack construction.
+
+6. Sharp two-component wedge realization. Fix beta >= 1 and a finite connected simplicial complex K_beta with |K_beta| homotopy equivalent to the wedge of beta copies of S^2. On the selected all-subdivision open-star basis site B_beta, let G be a finite abelian group. The following are equivalent:
+
+   (i) G is nonzero, d(G) <= 2 beta, and G is not cyclic of prime-power order;
+
+   (ii) on one selected finite open-star cover, the prescribed-realization prestack has two non-neutral component gerbes with nonzero homological images K_1,K_2 satisfying K_1 intersection K_2 = 0 and K_1 + K_2 = G.
+
+In that case Q_lab is isomorphic to G, Q_com=0, the kernel of Q_lab -> Q_com is G, and the constructed component presheaf has matching without amalgamation. This is explicitly presentation-relative and is not claimed to classify all gerbes, arbitrary presentations, or finite abelian groups independently of the constructed realization.
+
+7. Boundary with standard empirical models. For a finite empirical model e on a finite measurement set with finite nonempty outcome sets and a finite antichain of contexts covering the measurements, the terminally augmented support presheaf has
+
+   (aS_e)(X) = compatible supported families = globally supported assignments = global sections in the Abramsky-Brandenburger sense.
+
+Thus strong contextuality is absence of a matching family at this set-valued level, not MWA. For every supplied abelian sheaf A-script, Disc(aS_e) x B A-script is a canonical split banded stack lift and all its component gerbes are neutral. The Abramsky-Mansfield-Barbosa obstruction attached to a local section is instead a relative Cech H^1 torsor class; it cannot universally be recovered from a global-component-section map with component sheaf aS_e, because for a strongly contextual model that map has empty domain while local-section-indexed AMB classes remain. The finite-site homological images and wedge criterion therefore apply to an empirical model only after a band and a non-split prestack lift are supplied as extra data; they are not invariants of the bare empirical model.

Imported or essentially standard material includes: Giraud III.2.1.5.3, which says that a stack over its component sheaf is a gerbe and that pullback along a component section is the corresponding maximal subgerbe; Giraud's abelian-banded H^2 classification and neutrality criterion; local-object/overlap-arrow/triple-defect Cech cocycles and their change by coboundaries; realization of H^2-classes by abelian-banded gerbes; Leray and ordinary-nerve comparisons under the stated hypotheses; UCT naturality and its Ext kernel; finite Pontryagin duality; quotient universal properties; subgroup sum/intersection arithmetic and fibre counting; the structure theorem for finite abelian groups; and the standard split stack used at the empirical-model boundary.

+Our defensible paper-specific remainder is narrower: the exact representative-rigid terminal no-go formulation; the one explicit finite-label prestack that simultaneously controls the component presheaf, terminal essential surjectivity, neutral/non-neutral labels, and prescribed homological images; the exact negative empty-domain comparison with local-section-indexed AMB classes; and, at most, the exact two-label wedge realization criterion as a sharp corollary of that selected construction. The component-obstruction framework likewise overlaps the companion. The narrow lower-language separation is only in the technical appendix and is not being offered as a general logic theorem.
